## 3.4 CODE DAY 2 BUILD — THE OWL'S HOUSE IS BUILT (**STRATEGY ENTRY 7**, `2026-11-13`, owner's GO: "go … note all of these steps and system we designed in the holybook so you know how we build this and then continue step by step and make our app better and more clever with less work and easier")

🧸 **For a 3-year-old boy, the whole build as we designed it, step by step (the house, not the toys):**
- **STEP 0 — the rule before the rules.** Before any wall went up we asked the Book, not our feelings: *"answer from the Holy Book with citations before asking the owner anything it already records."* Every decision below has a file:line behind it.
- **STEP 1 — the shop, not the copy machine.** The new hall needs eyes, a brain, a notebook, a clock, ears, trap-sense. In the old factory every one of those says *"take my TWIN, not me"* — `import`, zero copied lines. Nothing in the old hall was edited (COMPANY 3 N1), so the old boat still sails byte-identical.
- **STEP 2 — the owl cannot see, so we bolted on the eyes.** `step1_data_acquisition.DataAcquisition` (`main.py:1040-1041` proves it stands alone; sources `bookmapbridge|demo`, `config.py:245-248`). One file, no threads of its own, no order button behind it.
- **STEP 3 — the owl cannot think alone, so the brain is on loan.** `step2_market_analysis.analyze_market` (`:6167`) does the level-3 porridge ONCE (it already knows `candles_tf`, the closed-bar clock, the notebook ingest). In the new hall the bucket's three numbers are COMPUTED AND WRITTEN DOWN for the twin book, and drive **nothing** — because a factory that can only write is a factory that can never hurt you.
- **STEP 4 — two notebooks, never one shared pencil.** `signal_team.get_book()` is a per-process singleton (`:935-939`) → factory 2 gets its OWN `LevelBook` in its own process. Same math, separate memory, zero contamination (that is why "share the notebook" was rejected).
- **STEP 5 — the six small helpers, one file each (`tools/gates_helpers.py`).** `RungState` (the toll book: closes-beyond / hold-minutes / volume-beyond / snap-back + `ACCEPTED` + `va_prior` from day 2), `find_gaps` (FVG learner, wick-to-wick, 1.0×ATR floor, retired by a close back inside), `Avwap` (learner, volume-fed only — no volume, no vote), `uob_check` (the magnet star: the birth bar printed ~zero tail volume), `session_note` (the anchor-under-the-feet card text, weight 0), `oi_read` (returns BLIND until `tools/oi_loader.py` is born). Plus `KIND_MAP`, because the old hall speaks `poc_day` and the ladder speaks `naked_poc` — an adapter, not a fight.
- **STEP 6 — the heartbeat (`factory2_gates.py`).** Wake only on a CLOSED M3 bucket; look → think → assemble `cyc` (the exact committee field table: `sigs, prev, cycle_id, levels, atr, price, judges, notes, toll, trap, l3q, oi, effort, prefire, symbol`) → `committee.committee_evaluate` → write **ONE** diary line → sleep. Exceptions write one named line and the loop lives (constitution law 8).
- **STEP 7 — the footprint law (the owner's worry, now a law).** *"after 2-3 months the diary must not eat my disk and CPU."* So: one ~1KB line per heartbeat (measured mean 756 B, max 820 B over 40 hearts), `fold_day()` gzips FINISHED days — **folded, never trimmed** — the toll book prunes rungs nobody touched for an hour (`prices` ring capped at 400), and a stale feed makes the loop idle instead of vote. Budget: ≤0.3 % of one core, measured by the teacher as `CPU TASTE` (`--runtime-seconds`).
- **STEP 8 — the door is still a door.** G1 broadcasts every cycle (silence forbidden), G2 names the rung under the feet, **G3 is the only voice that says BUY or SELL**, and votes are counted only there; one desk = one vote; BREAK needs the toll paid AND ≥3 desks (creak/tilt/iceberg/breadth) — `committee.py:314-324`. A verdict with no diary pen is REFUSED by name (`DIARY`), because no-diary-no-deal is law 6, not a suggestion.
- **STEP 9 — the replay-first law (the owner's re-order, and it made the build better).** Original plan: 30 demo days, then the sea. Owner: *"we DON'T WAIT FOR 30 DAYS!!!"* → the sea sails the **raw tapes the same night the tests pass** (`tools/gates_replay.py`), and the live days after that are plumbing proof only (feed ages, Windows file locks, restarts). WHY raw tapes and not the fat jar: `market_snapshot.json` is overwritten every cycle (`main.py:1088-1089`) and `snapshots_history.jsonl` carries no candle tails, so the toll would read 0 and BREAK could never fire — if the jar is ever used the line is stamped `replay:unplugged`.
- **STEP 10 — the sea itself.** `tools/gates_replay.py`: discover the day's tape (the old boat's own `audit_day.load_ticks` first, our reader as fallback), walk prints chronologically into causal M1 buckets (zero look-ahead), resample closed M3, feed the REAL `analyze_market(md, now=tc)` (the classroom precedent `tools/raw_replay.py:2329`), hand the snapshot to `factory2.run_cycle`, and grade the night. `--depth auto` also feeds the tape's `DepthBid/DepthAsk/Mbo` rows through a **temporary** `bookmap_bridge_provider._BridgeTail`/`_MboTail` (constructed with `__new__`, never started, never pointed at a file) so PUSH/desks are real; without them the card honestly says `PUSH=NONE` and the diary says why. Modes are stamped in every line: `replay:tapes`, `replay:jar`, `live`, `demo`.
- **STEP 11 — the night teacher in the office's own clothes.** `tools/gates_report.py` reads the diary (+ the sea's json) and writes `data/gates_<DATE>.txt` + `data/gates_<DATE>_metrics.json` (with a `"gates"` block) using `audit_day`'s exact row grammar (`[PASS]/[WARN]/[FAIL]/[--]` → what it measures → what happened → what it means): Section A diary health, B committee tallies, C twin scoreboard, D per-EYE grades at verdict episodes (+12m path — "a note serves the member or it's out"), E footprint, F the sea. It **never imports** `audit_day`, so an old-factory refactor can't take the new hall down. `report_evening_gates.sh` is the one evening command (sea → one heartbeat → teacher); `report_evening.sh` and the Report Office were NOT edited.
- **STEP 12 — the babies, not the fish.** The new factory's `shadow_naive` counterfactual babies (door-chasing banned; ladder-TP 4×0.5 ATR; `SL=2.0×ATR` fallback; escort laws: grace, ONE LAW never-widen-never-add, chandelier 1.5×ATR floor 1.2×ATR, break-even, time-stop 45 m M3-wave/12 m sprint, fire-escape −$12|0.25 %+3×ATR, losers only) are PAPER ONLY. Verified: `position_manager.py:1554` `CHANDELIER_WAVE_TRAIL`, `shooting_team.py:508-518` `BOOK_SHELTER`, Book:220/222/282. **The new hall has no hands: no `ALLOW_LIVE_TRADING`, no `GATES_VOICE`, no order client anywhere in its files** — a test proves it (`test_factory2_never_shares_the_notebook`).
- **STEP 13 — meadow first, always.** `test_factory2.py`: **38 tests, stdlib-only** (no numpy, no engine), so the hall is provable in a bare meadow. `python -m pytest -q test_factory2.py test_committee.py` → **55 passed** (38 new + the Code-Day-1 17, which still pass untouched). The end-to-end test literally runs the sea and the teacher as subprocesses and demands the report file - and one of the 38 is a **vocabulary guard** (L-C): every stamp word the hall or the sea can write must be in the teacher's known list, or the test fails. That new test caught a real gap in the vocabulary while being written (the sea's `l2:*` words were not in the list), which is Law #12 paying rent on day one.
- **STEP 14 — what "continue step by step" now means.** (1) owner applies the patch; (2) runs the tests; (3) sails the sea on his own raw tapes; (4) turns the hall on **diary-only** for 5-10 plumbing days; (5) then EVERY further step (L2 depth on, OI loader, `GATES_VOICE`, the swap) waits for its own yes. The owl's house has a door, a clock and a diary — and no order button, forever, unless the Book is amended.

- **And the fix does not rescue those two numbers:** your 11-12 tape's last row is 12:12Z UTC and the episode sat at 12:06Z - six minutes of future where nine are needed. That is DATA, not a bug, and the honest paper says `no future rows to be measured by` rather than inventing a result. To actually score toll on that day, sail a window whose tape outlasts it (a full closed day, `--window 08:00-22:00`), or wait for the live hall's own `data/factory2_toll.json`.
- **What this is and is not:** two `TOLL=unpaid` verdicts on 19 cycles of one day is **plumbing proof, not an edge claim**. The fire-law worked (`allow=1` on both), and the ladder refused to charge either one - designed behaviour, not a wound. `HEALTH: CHECK` is the honest grade for 19 cycles, and `VERDICT: SEA-GRADE ONLY (hall off)` stays until you sign.

**WHERE YOUR TAPES ACTUALLY LIVE (measured from the owner's packet `data/sea_read_20261113.txt`, HP-240 17:56 - written HERE because ledger items 21-23 and 25 all cost a whole sea before anyone knew it):**
- **The LIVE tail is NOT in `data/`.** The bridge resolves its own files next to its own module: `bookmap_bridge_provider.py:132-145` -> `os.path.join(here, "ticks.csv")`, `:161-175` -> `here/mbo.csv`, and `A:\gitHub\Gold-BookMap\mbo.csv` is even a HARDCODED candidate at `:164`. `here` = the repo root, so the file being written right now is `A:\gitHub\Gold-BookMap\ticks.csv` - and it holds only what survived the last fold (`:391/:403` rotate ticks by MB size, `:917` does the same for mbo). Measured: at 17:56 `data/` contained NO `ticks*`/`mbo*` file at all, while the sea at 17:24 had read 417,667 rows for "today" - both statements are true, and the second one is only true because of the root.
- **A closed day is a PILE of slices:** `data/archive/{ticks,mbo}_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.csv.gz`, 8-13 MB each, **236 files** on your rig (newest `ticks_20261113_164928.csv.gz`, 12,667,683 B). A slice's NAME-time is the FOLD moment, not its first row - measured `mbo_20261113_152540.csv.gz` beginning at `2026-11-12T21:53:08.688+01:00` - so only the ROW timestamps are truth. `iter_tape` filters per row and `break`s at the end bound, which is exactly what makes a spilling slice safe.
- **TWO tapes, TWO grammars, never interchangeable** (headers copied verbatim from your own files): ticks = `time,event,price,size,level,operation,instrument` - the SUPERSET (`Last` prints, `DepthBid/DepthAsk` book, `Bid/Ask` top, and `Mbo` rows whose `level` is `-1`); mbo = `time,event_type,order_id,price,size,instrument` - the ONLY place a real order id exists (measured `7816604578040`). Reading one with the other's columns is the item-25 fake-order trap. The bridge reads both itself (ticks `:274-332`, mbo `:720-760`), every row is stamped `+01:00`, and `bbp._parse_ts` remains the single clock (item 3).
- **Consequences we now CODE instead of remember:** a same-day sea is a tail slice and cannot warm up (item 23); the day LIST came from the archive (`rglob`, recursive) while the day's FILES did not (fixed globs) - that mismatch is the whole `0 verdicts` story (item 22); and an empty `ls data/ticks_*.csv` is NORMAL on your rig, which is why `src=` and `SEA FED BY:` now print it as a stamp instead of leaving it as a mystery. **(Code Day 3.4 added a fourth consequence, and it cost the first real tape night its whole grade):** a YOUNG day is the worst shape of all, because the only slice carrying today's rows is a fold that starts whenever the bridge last rotated - `ticks_20261113_164928.csv.gz` does not begin at 00:00, it begins mid-window - so the sea reads the day with NO history in front of it and the 60-candle law refuses every cell. The sea now prints the arithmetic of that on one line (`day math : window 08:00-12:00Z, tape first row 11:06Z, grid N cell(s), warm-up needs 60 candles -> tape starts 186min INTO the window`), because **a number without its window is a mystery waiting to be misread**.

**THE PACKET RITUAL (standing law of this section, so nobody re-invents it mid-run):** every long or risky command writes ONE `.txt` under `data/` and the owner attaches THAT file - never a screenshot, never "read my screen". Shape:
```bash
D=$(date +%Y%m%d); O=data/<name>_$D.txt
{ echo "# <NAME> rig=$(hostname) made=$(date -Is) git=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD) book=$(wc -l < HOLY_BOOK.md) python=$(python -V 2>&1)"
  <the commands, in order>
} > "$O" 2>&1          # use >> for a second block, so ONE file holds the whole story
tail -25 "$O"          # the owner reads this line back; I read the attached file
```
Five rules WE obey when writing any recipe for the owner: (1) **guard every possibly-empty variable** (`if [ -n "$f" ]`) and put `</dev/null` on any tool that takes a file name - an empty name makes `mawk`/`grep`/`sed` read the KEYBOARD and the window only looks frozen (item 28, it happened to the owner); (2) reading a folded slice needs `gzip -cd`, never `head`; (3) metadata first, bytes second - `ls`/`wc` cost nothing, a recursive `du` on a network folder does not belong in a recipe; (4) **one writer per night** into a shared `A:\gitHub\Gold-BookMap\data`, because the night papers are shared too; (5) every packet carries `git=` + `book=` + the quarantine line, so "which tree produced this?" is never a guess. Three rules the owner's packets ADDED the hard way: **(6) a packet's first line is read before its last one** - I compare its `sha`/`book=` values against my own CURRENT seal, and if they differ the step is not green even when the run says `55 passed` (item 30, a near-miss between two seals); **(7) never write `want <N>` from memory** - the `want` numbers must be copied from the seal of the artifact actually handed over, which is how `book AFTER : 3268 (want 3268)` was able to look perfect while 19 lines of Book were missing; **(8) a cap budgets FED cycles, not tape minutes** - since `gates.replay.2026-11-13.5` the 60-candle warming is charged OUTSIDE `--max-minutes` (item 31), so `--max-minutes 240` means "about 80 cycles of the day", not "80 minus the warm-up, which on an open day is none at all".

**SEA HOW-TO (the commands, and the two gotchas the ledger below caught):**
```bash
GATES_DATA_DIR=/path/to/your/bookmap/data bash report_evening_gates.sh --sea-once
bash report_evening_gates.sh --sea 3                            # last 3 recorded days
bash report_evening_gates.sh --sea-once --max-minutes 240       # small machine: RAM cap
python tools/gates_replay.py --synthetic --window 00:00-23:59 --max-cycles 480 --quiet   # no tapes -> meadow
python tools/gates_replay.py --date 2026-11-12 --data-dir data --depth auto --quiet      # the sea alone
```
**Warm-up law the sea obeys:** a cycle is only voted on when the walk has **60 closed M3 candles** behind it, so a 40-minute window with no prewarm correctly returns `cycles fed 0 (skipped/stale N)` - that is not a crash, that is the hall refusing to vote on scraps. A thin morning tape also trips `only X prints in window` (needs 600 prints; `--min-prints N` lowers it ON PURPOSE, never silently).
**(Correction, Code Day 3.4 - item 31.)** That sentence was honest when it was written and it is now **weaker than the truth**: the same mechanism ate the owner's first tape-fed night and printed `fed 0 (skipped/stale 18)` on a day with **147,849 rows in RAM**. Three guards live in code now, not in a paragraph: (i) the prewarm floor is **arithmetic** - `every_s * 60 / 60`, i.e. 180 min at `every 180`, because 60 M3 candles cannot exist in less; (ii) `--max-minutes` no longer charges the warming against the owner's budget (`cap_warmed`, printed as `+Nmin of warming NOT charged`); (iii) a refusal is filed under its **cause** (`warm-up / stale / no-eyes / brain / snapshot`), never inside a joint `skipped/stale` total.
`GATES_DATA_DIR` is the folder where `ticks_YYYYMMDD*.csv` live: the shell now hands it to the sea (it did not — ledger item 10) and forwards every flag it does not know (item 9). The teacher grades the sea's json **and** the hall's diary; the sentence `THE NEW FACTORY IS HEALTHY (sea passed)` requires both.

**THE FIRST REAL SEA — your rig, 2026-11-13 17:24 (the packet `data/sea_run_20261113.txt`, 137 lines, kept by the owner):** `bash report_evening_gates.sh --sea 5 --no-heartbeat` over the recorded days -> `5/5 day(s) sailed, 633 cycle(s), 0 verdict(s)`, `tape clock parsed by: ts:bookmap_bridge_provider._parse_ts`, `G2 with a rung: 633 (100%)`, rung spread `htf_poc / onl / value_edge / poc_day / vwap / ibl / onh / supply / demand / today_open` (no single level eating the day), `max closes beyond a rung in one hold: 105`, `longest hold: 318 min`, `NIGHT GRADE: 3 PASS / 1 WARN / 1 FAIL / 4 not-answerable`, and the first line out of the sea was `[factory2] data quarantine: borrowed writers -> A:\gitHub\Gold-BookMap\data\gates_sandbox (live session_levels.json / last_book_state.json untouched)`.
- **The quarantine proven in the hardest shape there is:** this was NOT two machines. `ps -W` + `Get-CimInstance` on `HP-240` showed `12764 C:\Bookmap\Python\tmp\GoldBridge\script.py 32132` (the bridge/recorder) and `13720 main.py --loop` (the live loop), both started `12:07/12:08`, on the SAME disk as the sea that began `17:24`; `ls data/` at 17:56 still showed the loop's own `last_book_state.json` and `session_levels.json` at `17:56` while our papers sat in `data/gates_sandbox/`. So 633 replayed cycles of a PAST day went through the borrowed brain on the same folder the live loop trades from, and the live level book did not move. **Two of my words are corrected here rather than in chat: "two-rig run" was wrong — one machine, two windows; and `100%` of the cycles standing on a rung was 100 % of a JAR-fed day, where a rung is cheap (items 22/26).**
- What the packet proved and what it could not: the toll CAN pay on your market (`105` closes beyond a rung, `318 min` hold — the fat jar never showed anything like it), the fire-laws held (`Nothing was traded`, `0` errors, no diary writes from the replay), and `0 verdicts` + the `[FAIL]` diary line were both **my** tap and **my** wording, not your market's fault. That sentence is now a receipt and not a promise: v7's own run says `L3Q=ok | PUSH=UP | TOLL=paid` on tape-shaped days (registry, Code Day 3.3).

*(ledger note: this section IS STRATEGY ENTRY 7 - it lives under COMPANY 3 because the second factory is what the entry built; read it together with ENTRY 5 (the scan) and ENTRY 6 (the swap map), which it now supersedes in SHAPE (no old-file edits at all) but not in CONTENT.)*
**L-E (BOLD-THE-MYSTERY law, the owner's order of this night, now Book Law #12):** every mistake, mystery or weak part is **bolded, reviewed the same day, and guarded** - never quietly fixed, never shrugged at. The mystery ledger of THIS episode (kept here because the law says the list must be written, not remembered):

1. **OUR OWN BUG (code):** `RungState.observe()` rewrote `side` before comparing it with the previous side, so `closes_beyond` never passed 1 -> `TOLL=paid` was impossible -> **BREAK could never exist**. Caught by `test_toll_needs_two_consecutive_closes` (it failed loudly), fixed, and 480 sea cycles now reach `closes = 48`. Guard: that test, forever.
2. **OUR OWN WRONG IDEA (test, not code):** we wrote a test expecting BREAK from "3 desks including `delta`". The frozen committee counts **creak + tilt + iceberg + breadth** (`committee.py:314-324`) and `delta` only names the direction. The TEST was corrected, the committee was NOT touched - a gate is never re-tuned to make a red test green.
3. **A REAL BUG FOUND BY A REAL TAPE (double-clock):** `parse_last()` re-parsed the timestamp with `strptime` formats while the tape rows are ISO with `+00:00`, and the bridge parses them with `_parse_ts` (naive = local, date-aware). Two clocks on one day = the whole tape "parsed" as 0 prints and every cycle would have read `stale eyes`. Fixed: the grid walker passes its epoch down and the **bridge's own parser is the single clock** (`tape clock parsed by : ts:bookmap_bridge_provider._parse_ts` is printed in every report now). Mystery still open: **any future tape tool must reuse `bbp._parse_ts` or `audit_day.parse_ts`, never write a third parser** (written here so it cannot be "forgotten").
4. **A MEMORY TRUTH WE TOLD YOU INSTEAD OF HIDING:** the sea keeps the day's rows in RAM. On a busy gold day `ticks.csv` + rotated chunks is ~1 GB of TEXT rows, so `--depth auto` holds millions of tuples. Mitigations now in the tool: single pass (no double read, dropped the second `raw_lines` list), `--max-minutes N` cap, bounded error lists, ring-capped candle store. Still a KNOWN LIMIT: on the smallest machines sail `--max-minutes 240` or `--depth off`. Not a bug, a **cost we named before you found it**.
5. **A REPORT NAMING MYSTERY:** the sea wrote `gates_replay_<day>.jsonl`, the teacher looked for `gates_shadow_<day>.jsonl` -> honest-looking `[FAIL] DIARY EXISTS` on a night that actually had a diary. Fixed with a documented fallback, and the report prints which file it graded (`diary: gates_replay_20261113.jsonl (962 lines)`).
6. **A SHELL TRAP THAT ALMOST BECAME YOUR BUG:** in the delivered step list we wrote `--date $(python -c "...open("data/...")...")` - nested quotes through a Windows shell = broken command. The owner's run is protected by a **real flag** instead (`gates_report.py --date` is now fed from the sea json in the shipped step list, no inline python quoting). Law for us: never hand the owner a command with nested quotes.
7. **AN HONEST ZERO (not a failure):** on a synthetic tape the door says `PUSH=NONE` and verdicts are 0 because there is no real order-book depth; on the depth fixture the sea produced **4 real verdicts** which the fire-law `L3Q` then REFUSED (`L3Q=1`) because the tape had no trustworthy L3 window. Both are the system working: a verdict that cannot show its receipts does not get a ticket. MYSTERY LEFT OPEN: **on your real tape we expect `L3Q=ok` sometimes - if the first sea shows 0 verdicts AND `l2:no-depth-rows-in-tape`, that means my tape finder is not seeing your rotated chunks, and the finder (not the committee) is what we fix.** **(CLOSED by your first real sea, 2026-11-13: the prediction came TRUE in two layers - items 22 and 24. The finder was indeed blind to `data/archive/`, and behind it a swallowed `AttributeError` made L3 empty even with the tape open. Both fixed; `L3Q=ok | PUSH=UP` is now measured on a tape that has an order file.)**
8. **AN UNVERIFIED ASSUMPTION, MARKED:** `tools/gates_report.py` claims `folded ~0.12` compression factor for the footprint projection - that is a standard json-lines estimate, **not measured on your machine**. Guard: measure it after the first real folded week (`gzip --list`) and we replace the number with your receipt.
9. **A FLAG THAT LIED:** `tools/gates_replay.py --write-diary` was **declared in argparse and never read**. The evening shell passes it, so the "hall's own diary name" was silently never used. Fixed (it now writes `gates_shadow_<day>.jsonl`, which is what its help text always promised), and guarded by `test_no_dead_flags_between_shell_and_sea`: **every flag the shell passes to the sea must be READ by the sea** — a dead flag is a lie in a CLI.
10. **A PATH THAT WAS NEVER READ:** the shell printed `--data-dir "$DATA_DIR"` from a variable **it never defined**, so `GATES_DATA_DIR=/x` did nothing for the sea — the sea looked in `data/`, found no tape, and reported "0 prints". Found by the simplest test in the world: run the command and read its output. Fixed: `DATA_DIR="${GATES_DATA_DIR:-$DATA_DIR}"`, exported, passed to the sea; guarded by `test_the_shell_passes_the_tape_folder_to_the_sea`.
11. **A SWALLOWED FLAG:** the shell answered unknown arguments with `[warn] ignoring unknown argument` and **dropped them**, so the RAM cap promised in the ledger itself (`--max-minutes 240`) would have evaporated before reaching the sea. Fixed: unknown flags are forwarded to the sea verbatim (with their values). Guard: the same test as item 10 asserts the swallow-the-argument line no longer exists.
12. **MULTI-DAY SEA DELIVERED NOTHING TO THE TEACHER:** `--sea 5` wrote one json per day (`gates_sea_20261113_2026-11-09.json`) and NOTHING at the plain `--json-out` path the shell hands the teacher — so five sailed days graded as "no --sea file given" and the night said NOT HEALTHY while the sea was fine. Fixed: `merge_days()` sums the per-day tallies (counters added, `toll_closes`/`toll_minutes` as max, episodes concatenated, `src`/`depth` joined) and writes them to the exact path the caller asked for. Guard: `test_multi_day_sea_delivers_one_json_to_the_teacher`.
13. **A HALF-PARSED DATE:** `available_days()` sliced 10 characters out of `ticks_20261112.csv` and produced the "date" `20261112.c`. Python 3.11+ `date.fromisoformat` is generous enough to ACCEPT it, the strict parse elsewhere was not — the tape was on disk and the sea reported "0 prints". Fixed with ONE grammar at the front door (`canon_day` / `day_token`: accept `YYYY-MM-DD` or `YYYYMMDD`, refuse everything else loudly; `--date garbage` now exits 2 with the sentence "refusing to guess"). Guard: `test_day_grammar_is_canonical_for_the_sea`. Same family as item 3: **a half-understood timestamp is worse than a refused one.**
14. **OUR DIARY LIVED IN YOUR FOLDER:** the sea wrote its diary next to `--data-dir`, i.e. into your tape folder, where the teacher and the fold never look. Fixed: papers go to the repo's `data/`, `--data-dir` is ONLY a tape source.
15. **A FLAG WE PASSED THAT WAS BETTER OFF UNPASSED:** the evening shell passed `--write-diary` to the sea, which means "mix the replayed cycles into the hall's OWN live diary" — the night report then counted 67 sea cycles as if they were live ones (it even said "3 mode(s)"). Now the sea writes its own `data/gates_replay_<day>.jsonl` and the hall's `gates_shadow_<day>.jsonl` stays live/demo only. Also: the shell **clears that day's sea json before sailing**, so a night with no tape cannot inherit yesterday's grade.
16. **A REPORT THAT SAID SOMETHING FALSE:** with `--sea-only` the teacher skipped Section A while still reading an auto-found diary for Sections B-D, and printed "no diary graded, none exists". Fixed: `--sea-only` reads no diary at all (unless you name one with `--diary`) and the header says `(not read - --sea-only)`.
17. **A TEST THAT ENCODED THE PLATFORM INSTEAD OF THE LAW (the owner's rig caught it):** `test_fold_never_trims_and_never_touches_today` asserted `gzip bytes == b'{"a":1}\n{"a":2}\n'`. On Windows a text-mode write ends lines with `\r\n`, so the file on disk was `b'{"a":1}\r\n...'` and the fold - which is **binary and byte-exact** (`open(p,"rb")` + `gzip.open(gz,"wb")`, `factory2_gates.py:421`) - faithfully copied what was there and my test cried FAIL. `2 failed, 45 passed` on your machine vs `47 passed` on mine. Fixed the way the law demands: the test now reads the file's OWN bytes and asserts the fold preserved THOSE (`assert raw == before, "the fold must copy history BYTE for BYTE"`), and `Pen` now pins `newline="\n"` so the diary has one byte grammar on every machine (L-A counts BYTES - an OS that quietly adds a CR to each line would make the footprint law a lie). Guard: `test_the_diary_pen_writes_one_byte_grammar`. *Lesson written down: a test must compare the LAW to the FILE, never to a string from our own platform's habits.*
18. **A NAMED FOLDER WAS TREATED AS A HINT:** `available_days()` merged the boat's own finder (and a recursive scan of the repo) into the day list even when the caller said `--data-dir /x`. On your rig the fixture's two days became `['2026-11-13','2026-11-12']` - today had no tape, so the sea sailed `1 of 2` and my multi-day test failed. Fixed: `scoped=True` whenever `--data-dir`/`GATES_DATA_DIR` is given, and then the days are **exactly** the days inside that folder. Reproduced both ways in the sandbox: unscoped `['2026-11-13','2026-01-06','2026-01-05']`, scoped `['2026-01-06','2026-01-05']`. Guard: `test_an_explicit_data_dir_scopes_the_day_search`.
19. **A GUARD THAT ENCODED THE SANDBOX (found by the owner's rig, 30 minutes after item 18):** `test_an_explicit_data_dir_scopes_the_day_search` asserted that the UNSCOPED finder returns the fake fixture day - true only on a machine whose repo has almost no tapes. The owner's `data/` holds 10 real days, `--days` caps the list at 9, so the fake day was cut and the test failed (`1 failed, 48 passed`) while the *scoped* contract it exists to protect passed cleanly. Fixed: the test now asserts a **relation** (`set(scoped) <= set(wide)`, descending, ISO) with a cap big enough to never cut (999), and re-verified under BOTH conditions - 10 real days planted in `data/` -> `32 passed`, empty `data/` -> `32 passed`. *Law for every future guard, written here so it is not re-learned: **a test must assert a relation that holds on any machine, never a snapshot of the machine it was written on.*** Same family as item 17 (LF vs CRLF) and item 18 (one folder vs the whole repo): the sandbox is not the world.
20. **THE BORROWED BRAIN WRITES - so a replay can poison the LIVE rig (found by the owner's question "may I sail the sea on Windows-2 while the loop runs on Windows-1?"):** `analyze_market` is not a pure function. `step2_market_analysis.py:6693-6696` calls `session_levels.update(price, config.DATA_DIR, now=now)`, and `session_levels.py:119-186` **rewrites `data/session_levels.json`**, ROLLING the session whenever the day of the passed `now` differs from the file's day; `step2_market_analysis.py:5275-5282` + `:5942` write `data/last_book_state.json`. Replaying 2026-11-11's tape through the same brain therefore sets today's session to a replay day - measured in the sandbox with the quarantine switched off: `LIVE data/session_levels.json -> day=2026-11-11 open=4119.1 high=4153.6 prev_day=2026-11-12` (a FUTURE day as `prev_day`!). Those numbers are `pdh/pdl/prev_settle` for the level book the live G2 stands on. Fix (and it obeys law N1: the old factory's files are bit-identical, we touch only OUR process): `factory2_gates.isolate_data_dir()` moves `config.DATA_DIR` to `data/gates_sandbox/` at the very start of BOTH entry points (hall `main()` and `tools/gates_replay.py:main()`), so every borrowed writer lands in our own drawer; `GATES_SHARED_DATA=1` opts out on purpose. The same trick is what `conftest.py` already does for pytest ("running pytest on a trading machine can NEVER create or mutate live state/log files inside ./data") - we simply were not doing it for our own long-running replay. Measured after the fix: `LIVE session_levels.json / last_book_state.json: ABSENT`, `SANDBOX: day=2026-11-11 ...` - the replay kept its history to itself. Guard: `test_the_borrowed_brain_is_quarantined_from_live_data` (it re-runs the write and fails if the live file moves by even one byte). **Two-rig rule, written so nobody has to remember it: never run anything from either factory against a `data/` folder that a live loop is writing - unless the quarantine is on, which it now is by default.**
21. **A FILE THE SHELL READ AND NOTHING EVER WROTE (closed now, open since day one):** `report_evening_gates.sh` has tailed `data/gates_sea_err.txt` in its "the sea did not finish" warning since the FIRST draft, and `grep -rln gates_sea_err` across the whole repo returned exactly one file - the shell itself. So the warning could only ever print `its own words: ` and nothing after it, which reads like "clean" and means "silent". Fixed in v7: BOTH sea branches redirect `2> "data/gates_sea_err.txt"`, and the file is deleted before sailing (a stale trace from an older night must never be read as tonight's). Proof: after a clean 3-day archive sea the file exists and is 0 bytes - silence that is now EVIDENCE, not a hope.
22. **THE SEA WAS BLIND TO WHERE YOUR TAPES ACTUALLY LIVE (the root cause of `0 verdicts` on your first real sea):** `discover_tape()` searched `data/`, the repo root, `Workspace/data` and the sub-folders `raw`/`bookmap`/`tapes` - **never `data/archive/`**, which is where your bridge folds a closed day (`bookmap_bridge_provider.py:403/:917` rotate at ~100 MB into `ticks_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.csv.gz`). Cruelly, `available_days()` DID see them (it uses `rglob`, which is recursive), so the day appeared in the list and then had "no tape" - the sea fell back to the audit jar and printed `src=tapes:audit_day+no-tape-for-depth`, `depth feed: depth:off(no raw rows)`, `G3 real verdicts: 0`. Your own tools never made that mistake (`tools/raw_replay.py:224` searches `data_dir / "archive"`, `tools/backtest.py:728` globs `mbo_*.csv.gz`) - we wrote a second, worse finder. Fixed: `archive` is in the sub-folder list for BOTH kinds, and the line filter now also accepts bare `Bid`/`Ask` rows, because the bridge reads them at `:304/:307` while v6 dropped them - the top of the book never moved. Guard: `test_the_archive_tape_and_the_order_tape_are_both_found`.
23. **A LIVE DAY IS ONLY THE TAIL OF THE DAY:** your 2026-11-13 line read `tape rows in RAM 417667 prints 4556` and `cycles fed 0 (skipped/stale 144)` - not a crash, two refusals doing their job (`tools/gates_replay.py:479` stale eyes, `:493` fewer than 60 closed candles). Reason: the loose `ticks.csv` is whatever survived the LAST fold (yours had been rolled at 16:49, so it held ~40 minutes), and 40 minutes can never warm a 60-candle walk. So **sailing "today" means sailing the last slice** - and after item 22's fix the same command reads the day's folded slices too, which is why the fixture day in the v7 receipt below fed 31 cycles with `skipped/stale 0`. Law written: *a same-day sea is a warm-up race against the folder, not against the market.*
24. **THE DEEPEST ONE: L3 WAS EMPTY EVEN AFTER THE TAP WAS OPENED (a swallowed `AttributeError`).** After fixing the finder, a real order tape was read - 1,982 rows per day into the borrowed `_MboTail` - and the cards STILL said `L3Q=unavailable | PUSH=NONE`. Cause: `_l3_events()` chose the busiest instrument with `max(..., key=lambda kv: kv[1].line_count)`, but `_InstrumentState` is a `__slots__` class (`bookmap_bridge_provider.py:186-189`) with **no `line_count`** - that attribute lives on the TAIL, not the state. So every call raised `AttributeError`, our own `except (ValueError, AttributeError)` swallowed it, and the sea silently sailed blind. Measured in isolation: `hasattr(_InstrumentState(), "line_count") -> False`. Fixed: rank by `len(mbo_events)` and the seam now RETURNS its error (`_l3_events -> (events, err)`), which the sea prints as `L3 WIRING ERROR` and stores as `l3_errors` - a wiring bug may never again hide behind a safety net (same family as Code Day 3.2's swallowed `NameError`). Proof, same fixture before/after: `L3Q=unavailable, PUSH=NONE` -> **`L3Q=ok` and `PUSH=UP` on 93/93 cycles, `TOLL=paid`, `EXCESS=c42/h0m`**. Guards: `test_l3_events_survives_the_tail_it_borrows` (unit, no subprocess) + the end-to-end assertion that a card carries `L3Q=ok`. *And a note the owner will want: the fixture's `tc` must be derived from `bbp._parse_ts`, never from the machine's clock - `_parse_ts` localises a naive stamp to the MARKET zone, so `datetime.timestamp()` was one hour off and windowed every order out. An hour of clock error hides in five minutes of window.*
25. **TICKS-TAPE `Mbo` ROWS ARE FAKE ORDERS, and v6 was about to trade on them:** your `ticks.csv` carries `time,event,price,size,level,operation,instrument` with rows like `...,Mbo,3904.2000,1.0000,-1,BID_NEW,...` - `level` is `-1`, there is NO order id. `_MboTail._handle_line` falls back to `operation` as the order id (`:740-746`), so EVERY order would have been keyed by the literal word `BID_NEW`: thousands of "icebergs" from one string. The bridge already names this exact bug in its own comment - `bookmap_bridge_provider.py:334`: *"v5.3 fix: skip invalid order_ids from ticks.csv Mbo fallback (level=-1 causes 929 fake icebergs)"*. v7 refuses the shortcut: ticks-tape `Mbo` rows are counted (`2973 ticks-tape Mbo row(s) ignored as fake-id`) and **never** fed; the order tape (`mbo.csv` / `mbo_*.csv.gz`) is the ONLY source of orders in either factory, and a cycle without one is stamped `l3q:unavailable(no order tape)` (a new signed stamp word, so an L1/L2 vote can never be read as an L3 vote). Guard: `test_a_ticks_tape_alone_can_never_invent_orders`.
26. **THE NIGHT CALLED JAR CANDLES "A SEA":** your first real grade said `THE TOLL CAN PAY ON TAPES: max closes beyond a rung 105, longest hold 318 min` and it was TRUE - but four of the five days were fed by `audit_day`'s candles, not by raw tape, and the row never said so. New row **`TAPE vs JAR`** (grades itself, measures the source): `3 raw-tape day(s), 0 jar day(s), 3 day(s) with an order tape`, and the toll row now ends with either `measured on N raw-tape day(s)` or **`MEASURED ON JAR CANDLES, not raw tape - the tape proof of this number is still owed`**. The sea prints the same truth itself: `SEA FED BY: 3 raw-tape day(s), 0 jar day(s) ..., 2973 ticks-tape Mbo row(s) ignored as fake-id`. Law: **a number is worth its source; name the source on the line that carries the number.**
27. **`[FAIL] DIARY EXISTS` ON A NIGHT THAT WAS NEVER SUPPOSED TO HAVE A DIARY:** you ran `--no-heartbeat` on purpose (the live loop was trading in window 1), so no `gates_shadow_20261113.jsonl` could exist - and the teacher shouted FAIL, making a clean tape night read like an illness. Fixed: the shell passes `--hall-off` when it skipped the heartbeat, `DIARY EXISTS` abstains (`[ -- ]`) with the sentence *"not asked to: --no-heartbeat (hall off)"*, and the night closes with `VERDICT: SEA-GRADE ONLY (hall off) - ... no health certificate is given or withheld`. Deliberately in BOTH directions: a night with the hall off cannot be declared healthy, and cannot be declared ill. Guard: `test_a_hall_off_night_is_not_illness` (asserts `rc==0` with the flag, `rc==1` and a real FAIL without it).
28. **ONE OF MY RECIPES ATE YOUR TERMINAL:** the reading block I handed you ended in `awk -F, ... "$T"` with `T` empty (no `data/ticks_*.csv` exists - see item 22), and `mawk` treats an empty file operand as **stdin**, so the window waited for your keyboard and looked frozen. Measured here: `printf 'a,1\nb,2\n' | awk -F, ... ""` printed `saw:2=1`, i.e. it read the pipe, not any file. Law for every recipe we hand the owner: **guard every possibly-empty variable (`if [ -n "$f" ]`) and put `</dev/null` on any text tool that takes a file name - a missing file must end the line, never eat a terminal.** (Also standing: `head -2` of a folded slice needs `gzip -cd`.)
29. **A GUARD THAT SWALLOWED ITS OWN NUMBER (found by RUNNING the shipped shell, not by reading it):** to keep a bare date from being eaten as a day count, `--sea)` refused any value whose first character was `2` - so **`--sea 2`, `--sea 20`, `--sea 21`... all silently became `--days 3`, and the orphan number was forwarded to the sea as a positional**, which made argparse die with `error: unrecognized arguments: 2` while the evening printed only a one-line warn and graded an empty night. Your `--sea 5` worked because 5 does not start with 2 - which is precisely the kind of bug that waits for the day you sail `--sea 2`. Fixed: a count is now recognised by being **all digits and <= 99**, and a date (it contains `-`) falls through to the date case untouched. Guard: `test_the_shell_understands_every_sea_count` runs the shipped shell against a stub `python` on `PATH` and reads the ACTUAL command line for `--sea 2 / 20 / 5 / (nothing) / 2026-11-13 / --sea 3 <date>`, demanding no orphan digit anywhere; mutation-checked - with the old guard restored it fails with the exact leaked `2` in its message. *Standing rule, same family as items 9/10/11: a guard must reject the WRONG THING, not a look-alike of it.*
30. **A NEAR-MISS BETWEEN TWO SEALS (a process bug in MY side of the swap, written because LAW #13 says the list includes my process, not just the code):** I re-sealed v7 four times in one sitting (each time the Book grew: `490b1400` -> `d71983d0` -> `a43145a3` -> `f9628461`), and the owner applied **the third** because their terminal still held the recipe from that message - `book AFTER : 3268 (want 3268)` was green, the meadow said `55 passed in 28.54s`, and NOTHING was wrong except that 18 lines of Book (LAW #13, the tape map, the packet ritual) had not arrived. **What caught it in one second was the ritual itself: the packet's first lines print `v7 sha: a43145a3...`, so a seal can be compared instead of trusted.** Three rules, kept from now on: (a) **one paper per night** - when a patch is re-sealed, the older copy is deleted here AND the owner is told in the same breath; (b) a step is only called green after I have read the `sha`/`book=` lines of the owner's packet against my own seal, never after the owner's own `want` text matches (the `want` was mine and it was stale too); (c) a Book-only delta ships as its OWN tiny patch (`v7b`, one file) rather than asking the owner to reverse a 260 KB paper they already applied - `git apply -R` of a file they no longer hold is how a tree gets broken.
    *The pattern in 5, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 22, 24, 26, 27, 28, 31, 32: every one of these was invisible to the tests we had, because each test graded one file in isolation. The bug lived in the **seam between** two files - or, in item 24, in a `try/except` that made the seam QUIET. Lesson kept: **a shell and the tool it calls are one product — assert their seam, or run them end-to-end once and read the words.** And: a borrowed object's attributes must be READ, not assumed - `__slots__` classes do not forgive a guessed name.*
    *The second pattern, new in 22-28 and worth more than any of them: **your rig is the test we did not have.** Six of the eight were found by a real packet, a real `ls`, or a real freeze - not by me. Keep sending them.*

31. **THE WARMING ATE THE OWNER'S FIRST TAPE-FED NIGHT, AND THE GRADE BLAMED THE SEA (a REAL-RIG finding: no fixture of mine could have produced it):** the packet of 18:43 printed `day 2026-11-13 (src=tapes:own+mbo)` / `cycles fed 0 (skipped/stale 18)` / `tape rows in RAM : 147849` - **147,849 rows read, zero cycles voted** - and the teacher's card said `SEA RAN: FAIL`. Three of my own shortcomings sat inside that one line; all three are fixed in `gates.replay/report.2026-11-13.5` and each is pinned by a test. **(a) no cause.** every refusal (`warm-up`, `stale eyes`, `no eyes yet`, `brain`, `snapshot`) went into ONE counter called `errors`, so the paper said "skipped/stale" and I read it as "the tape is stale"; the counters are now split (`skip_kinds`) printed per cause and carried through `merge_days` into the json the teacher reads. **(b) a law with no arithmetic.** the sea requires 60 closed M3 candles before it votes (correct, kept) while its default `--prewarm` was 150 min - at `every 180` the law needs **180**, so a day whose tape starts late can NEVER warm up inside the window; the floor is now computed as `every_s * 60 / 60` and a test greps that expression, so the law, the test and this page must move together (LAW #13). **(c) a budget with the wrong shape.** `--max-minutes 240` capped the WINDOW, so the first cold hour of the owner's "240 minutes" was spent standing still - and a cap ending before the tape began made `events` EMPTY and crashed inside the grid line (`IndexError` - found by MY FIRST RUN of the v8.1 test, which is precisely the class of bug I promised to stop shipping). Now the warming is charged OUTSIDE the budget (`cap_warmed`, printed as `+Nmin of warming NOT charged`), and a cap that misses the tape is refused out loud: `NOT RUN: the cap (60min) ends before the tape's first row (11:06Z ... a slice's name is its FOLD moment, Book §3.4)`. **And the grade learned manners:** `SEA RAN` was `PASS if cycles >= 20 else FAIL`, so a night with two order books of 2.18M and 3.39M rows, `depth:live-from-tape` and `G2 with a rung 10/10` was called ill. It now reads PASS at >= 20, `WARN` "thin SAMPLE - proves plumbing, cannot grade a market" below that, `--` "NOT ILLNESS, NOT HEALTH: every cell was refused before the 60-candle law" for a cold start, and keeps FAIL only for "no rows at all" or "no cell fed and warm-up does not explain it". *Kept because it is this whole entry in miniature: the code was right, the PAPER was silent, and silence gets read as the worst available meaning.* **One more of my own, filed in the same item so it is not hidden:** my first version of the NEW card handed `Teacher.add()` the literal grade `"--"` instead of the house word `NA`, and the report died on `KeyError` (`gates_report.py:71-72` is a closed vocabulary ON PURPOSE) - caught only because a test RUNS the teacher, never because the code was read. **Guard added the same day:** a test that drives a tape starting 186 min into the window and demands `warm-up 19`, `stale 0`, the `day math` line and the `NOT RUN` refusal - written with `LOCAL_TZ` PINNED, because item 19 is still alive: `bookmap_bridge_provider.py:75-77` stamps naive tape rows with `ZoneInfo(LOCAL_TZ or Europe/Budapest)`, so an unpinned fixture slides 8 hours depending on the box (my first version of this test was wrong for exactly that reason, and it said so in a traceback rather than a lie).
32. **TWO FIXTURES, ONE FILE NAME (a test bug that made a green test lie):** the cold-open-day test wrote both of its scenarios into the SAME `tmp` dir under the SAME slice name `ticks_YYYYMMDD_235900.csv.gz`, so the second day silently OVERWROTE the first and the third assertion graded a tape it never asked for - and the number still looked plausible (`1 cell refused`), which is the worst kind of wrong. Fixtures are now one directory per scenario (`sub="cold"` / `sub="warm"`) and the helper passes that dir as `--data-dir`. **A test that overwrites its own evidence is the same crime as a report that swallows a cause: both make a lie look tidy.** Guard: every fixture helper takes a scenario name and creates its own subtree.
33. **A TEST THAT REMEMBERED INSTEAD OF MEASURING (green on my box, red on the owner's):** `test_a_cold_open_day_says_WARMING...` asserted `warm-up == 19` and the literal string `tape starts 186min INTO the window`. On Linux that is what the fixture produces; on `HP-240` the same code printed a different lead-in, because a naive tape stamp is resolved by `bookmap_bridge_provider._parse_ts:75-77` through `ZoneInfo(LOCAL_TZ or Europe/Budapest)` — and pinning `LOCAL_TZ=UTC` in the child's environment is NOT enough to make my fixture's arithmetic identical on both boxes (item 19's family, one generation later: I pinned the CLOCK but still hard-coded the ANSWER). **Fix, and the rule:** the test now DERIVES everything from what the sea reports — `window.cells`, `window.first_row`, `window.win_start` — and asks the only question that matters: *was every refused cell blamed on the warming, out loud?* (`sk["warm-up"] == cells`, `sk["stale"] == 0`, and the printed number matched by regex rather than by a remembered value). **Standing rule: a test may pin an input's SHAPE; it must not memorise a DERIVED number.** If a test and the code can disagree only because of the machine, it is a machine test, not a behaviour test. Guard: the test now also asserts the per-day paper EXISTS (`assert w`), because deriving from nothing silently returned to hard-coding (see 34).
34. **THE AGGREGATE ATE THE DAY'S OWN PAPER:** `tools/gates_replay.py` writes the day's json to `--json-out` inside `replay_day`, and then, for a SINGLE-day sail, `main()` re-opened the SAME path and wrote the merge over it. So `window`/`day math` — the very numbers item 31 introduced so a human could check the arithmetic — were unreadable from the file every test and the owner were told to read, and my first v8.2 test failed with `window == {}` before I found out why. **Fix:** the sea now also writes `<name>.days.json` (a list, one entry per day, refused days included), for one day or many; the aggregate keeps its path so the teacher is unchanged. **The lesson is the same one as item 5 (`gates_replay_*.jsonl` vs `gates_shadow_*.jsonl`): when two writers share a path, the reader is not told which one spoke — so give each paper its own name, never let one land on top of the other.** Also stamped in the same breath: the aggregate now carries `printed_by: <REPLAY_VERSION>`, because a json without a version is a rumour.
35. **MY RECIPE WAS THE BUG, AND THE NEW REFUSAL CAUGHT ME:** the plumbing check I handed over was `--sea 1 --no-heartbeat --max-minutes 60 --min-prints 20`. On a rig at 20:13 the only tape for `2026-11-13` is a fold starting 11:07Z, so a 60-minute window measured from the 08:00 boundary contains **no rows at all** — and v8.1's new guard said so instead of crashing or feeding zero silently. Two rules come out of it: **(a) never give the owner a command whose parameters were chosen in MY sandbox shape** — a cap that is comfortable for a closed synthetic day is impossible for an open real one; the correct plumbing check on their rig is `--sea 2 --no-heartbeat --max-minutes 240` (yesterday is CLOSED, its tape begins at the window edge). **(b) a refusal must carry its own instruction:** the card now prints `READ THIS AS: the sea was ASKED FOR NOTHING` + the command to use instead, because a FAIL without advice sends the owner hunting for a broken thing that is not broken. **Related and bolder: my `ls -l data/session_levels.json` "quarantine check" was WRONG** (that file belongs to their live loop and is always there) — the honest check is `ls -l data/gates_sandbox/` plus `stat -c '%y %s' data/session_levels.json` BEFORE and AFTER our run: if the live file's mtime/size does not move while our writers land in the sandbox, the quarantine held. A check that can only fail teaches the reader to distrust green.)
36. **I DELETED A TEST WITH A STRING SURGERY AND ONLY THE COUNT NOTICED:** while rewriting the cold-open-day test I replaced everything between two `def test_` anchors and silently swallowed `test_the_sea_grade_names_a_cause_before_it_names_a_verdict` — the four-shape table for the new card. The suite answered `57 passed`, not `58`, and THAT one line was the catch: restored from `git show aebcff1:test_factory2.py`. **Standing rule for any whole-block edit in this house: print `grep -c '^def test_'` before and after, and the registry line in the Book must state the count, so a vanished test is impossible to miss.** *It belongs here because the same night already produced item 30 (four seals, one stale paper) and item 32 (two fixtures sharing a name): my tooling is a factory too, and a factory that eats its own work reports green.*
37. **OUR SANDBOX HOLDS A MIRROR OF THE LIVE DRAWER, AND THAT IS THE POINT (a design note your packet forced me to write down):** `data/gates_sandbox/` contains `session_levels.json` (358 B) and `last_book_state.json` (63 B) - the SAME names as the live files the trading loop uses. Read naively that looks like the quarantine wrote into the live drawer. It did not: `isolate_data_dir()` redirects OUR process's `config.DATA_DIR`, and the borrowed brain needs those exact names to do its normal thing (`step2_market_analysis.py:6693-6696` -> `session_levels.update(...)`), so it makes them in OUR drawer and your live file never moves. **The proof is the pair of lines the packet now prints** - `LIVE-level BEFORE/AFTER` (mtime + size): equal size and a timestamp that advances only with your loop means the wall held. The law behind it: a sandbox that does NOT look like the real thing is not a rehearsal.
38. **THE TEACHER SCORED A SILENCE (found by your first verdicts, by no test of mine):** `0/2 episode(s) went the way the door said` was printed from two episodes whose `fwd9` was `None`. The line was `hits = sum(1 for e in eps if (e.get("fwd9") or 0.0) > 0 and "BOUNCE" in ... or (e.get("fwd9") or 0.0) < 0 and "BREAK" in ...)` - `None` became `0.0` through `or 0.0`, `0.0 < 0` is False, and the missing measurement was counted as the door being wrong. Two faults sat behind it: **the cap truncated the tape as well as the cells**, so a verdict near the end of a `--max-minutes 240` day literally had no +9 minutes to be measured by; and **the boolean had no parentheses**, so `A and B or C and D` would also have let a BOUNCE match a NEGATIVE number. Fixes: the sea reads `look_min = 10` minutes of rows past the last cell (the pointer still stops at `tc`, so look-ahead rows are looked at, never fed), prints `cells to HH:MMZ` beside the tape end, and the report scores `_way(e)` as `yes / no / none` - an all-`none` night ABSTAINS with `NOT MEASURED, NOT FAILED`. **The law: `or 0.0` on an optional measurement is the same crime as a swallowed exception (item 24) - it converts I DO NOT KNOW into a number, and numbers get traded.** Guards: `test_a_verdict_near_the_end_of_a_capped_day_still_gets_its_horizon` (a verdict inside the last nine minutes of the CELLS gets a horizon when the TAPE has one) and `test_a_missing_horizon_is_named_and_never_scored` (this packet's exact `fwd9=None` pair must abstain; fed `-4.5/+3.0` the same episodes must read `1/2 MEASURED`).
39. **I FIXED A TEST BY MAKING IT SHORTER INSTEAD OF STRONGER (item 33's second form - caught on my box this time, but only because I copied your method instead of my memory):** to escape the Windows/Linux clock argument I moved the cold-open-day fixture to a FIXED calendar day with ABSOLUTE stamps (`...+01:00`, like your real tapes) and no `now()`. Good. Three things inside that rewrite were wrong, and the DERIVED assertions exposed each one in a single run: **(a)** my first copy wrote the SAME timestamp on all 1400 rows (`first_utc` inside the loop, no `+ 20*i`), so the warming test measured a six-minute tape and reported `stale 17, warm-up 2` - a WRONG-BUT-PLAUSIBLE pair that a hard-coded `== 19` would never have distinguished from a machine difference; **(b)** I then asserted day 2's warm-up was `<=` day 1's cell count - two different days compared by one number, failing `20 <= 19`; **(c)** the `win_end` bound became ambiguous the moment my own look-ahead made it `12:10Z` while `cells_end` was `12:00Z`. The rule that came out is now how every sea test in this house is written: **pin an INPUT, assert RELATIONS the paper states (causes add up to the total; nothing is blamed on `stale`; the printed number equals the counted number) - never carry the sea's arithmetic in the test's head.** Also re-stamped: my block surgery deleted `test_the_sea_grade…` for two edits and the name-diff caught it (item 36 again); the file is 41 -> 43, and `60 passed` with the committee. **What item 39 could NOT see, and what the same night then found: the clock argument was real but it was not why YOUR box went red - that was item 41. Two wrong diagnoses of one failure, both of them mine, and the second one only died when I stopped reading my own sandbox as the world.**
40. **THE OPEN DAY WILL KEEP SAYING `fed 0` UNTIL IT IS CLOSED, AND THAT IS NOT AN ERROR (so nobody loosens a law to make the red go away):** your 11-13 line read `warm-up 18` with `tape starts 187min INTO the window`. A day whose first row is 11:07Z cannot own 60 candles before 12:07Z, and the cap stops the cells at 12:00Z. The honest answers: sail a CLOSED day (`--sea 2` by evening), widen the cap (`--max-minutes 480`), or start the window after the tape (`--window 12:00-23:00`). **What is forbidden is the fourth option - lowering `60`** - because that number is not decoration: it is the M3 warm-up law, and a committee that votes on 20 candles votes on noise. A green bought by weakening a law is the most expensive colour in this house.
41. **THE LEAK: A TEST POINTED AT A TEMP DRAWER STILL DRANK FROM THE REPO'S OWN (this is what your `1 failed, 57 passed` actually was - reproduced here on 2026-11-13 and closed in `gates.replay.2026-11-13.7`):** `discover_tape()` searched `(data_dir, root_dir, root_dir/Workspace/data)`. `available_days()` had been scoped to a named `--data-dir` since item 19, but **the file search had not**, so a test that sailed a fixture in `/tmp` still found the real slices in the repo's `data/archive/` whenever a day name collided. My sandbox has no `data/archive/` at all; your rig has 236 files in it. Proof, both directions, run here: at `5282af4` inside a repo-shaped tree with four archive slices for the colliding days, **`2 failed`** (`test_a_cold_open_day…`, which is exactly your red line, and `test_a_ticks_tape_alone_can_never_invent_orders`, which reported **211 order rows that do not exist in its own fixture** - 211 rows of YOUR mbo tape); in a clean tree the same two tests are `2 passed`. After the fix: **`61 passed` in the rig-shaped tree and `61 passed` in the clean one**, i.e. the same paper on both machines regardless of what the drawer holds. The fix is one line and a flag: `bases = (data_dir,) if scoped else (data_dir, root_dir, ...)` with `scoped = bool(a.data_dir)`, and a new `tape scope : explicit --data-dir ONLY (repo drawer not searched)` line on the paper, because a scope you cannot see is a scope you will violate. `--root-dir` also got real (the shell had been forwarding it into `unrecognized arguments` since v7 - item 15's pattern, and the reason the test can build a fake repo at all). Guard: `test_a_named_data_dir_shuts_the_repo_s_own_drawer` asserts BOTH halves - a named `--data-dir` must not see the repo's archive (your tests), and NO `--data-dir` must still search it (your real sail) - and it is mutation-tested: deleting the scoping line brings `211` back. **The general law, over item 19 rather than beside it: scoping the ANSWERS and not the INPUTS is not isolation; every finder in a tool must honour the same `scoped` flag or none of them do.** Consequence for how I debug from now on: a packet that says `1 failed` with no assertion text is worthless as evidence, which is why this version's recipe stopped piping pytest through `tail -3` - the destroyed line was the one that would have told me this four hours earlier.
42. **THE MAPPER DRAWS ONLY NEARBY FIGHT LINES - the owner's law, `+-80 USD` (2026-11-13 21:00, given in the three-friends register: "the market is so big and will be so hard"; implemented in `gates.replay.2026-11-13.8` / `gates.report.2026-11-13.8`):** the sea now trims the borrowed brain's map (`signal_map.targets` + `memory_doors`, the two lists `factory2_gates.levels_from_view` builds rungs from) to whatever stands within `--rung-band` USD of the last print, default **80** (on a ~4,000 gold that is +-2%), and prints the cut on every paper: `mapper band : +/-80 USD of the last print; lines kept 846 of 846`, and the night teacher repeats it under G2 - because a `G2 with a rung 0/141` without that line reads as "the market was quiet" when it means "we refused to look that far". Three things this taught me about my assumptions, all measured rather than argued: **(a)** on calm gold the band RARELY changes a verdict, because `committee.CUTOFF_ATR = 2 x ATR` (a few USD in quiet weather) already refuses far rungs geometrically - at `+-2 USD` the map fell 846 -> 287 lines and G2 still named a rung on **141/141** cycles; so what the owner's law buys is the DRAWING (a small, legible, cheap sheet), not a new signal; **(b)** an empty sheet is **named, never skipped**: my first design refused the cycle (`_skip("band")`), and that would have quietly starved `RungState` - the rung memory is fed inside `run_cycle`, so a skipped cycle loses an `accepted` observation and turns a perf shortcut into a silent history bug (item 24's shape: a fast wrong thing). Now: `band_empty` counts cycles the band left with nothing, and the note `band:0/N(nothing near)` goes on the cycle's own paper; **(c)** the filter lives in `tools/gates_replay.py`, NOT in `committee.py`/`factory2_gates.py`, for a reason worth repeating: those two are files the owner's tree carries UNTRACKED from a cumulative patch, so any hunk against them fails `git apply` the moment their local copy drifts by one byte - and `git apply` is atomic, which would cost them the whole night's paper for a cosmetic edit. The same law belongs in `committee.build_rungs` when the hall goes live, and it will ship as its own paper with its stamp verified first. Guards: `test_the_mapper_band_is_a_pure_cut_of_the_map` (the arithmetic, the in-place trim, `0` and negative = OFF-not-erase, and the empty-sheet case the sea cannot force - a brain always keeps a line AT the print, so `+-0.001` still keeps 141 lines) and `test_an_empty_band_starves_nothing_and_says_so_on_the_paper` (printed numbers == json numbers, the day is still walked, `map empty` may not be printed when lines were kept, and a wider band can never keep fewer lines).
43. **THE OWNER OFFERED TO STOP THE LIVE LOOP FOR A REAL-FEED TEST; I DECLINED, AND THE REASONS ARE THE KIND WE FORGET (2026-11-13 21:00 Budapest, two hours before Friday's gold close):** (1) the loop is what keeps the tails alive - stopping it freezes the very files a live sail would read, and our own stale-feed law would then refuse the night as `stale`, which is the correct answer to a wasted evening; (2) the quarantine proof NEEDS the loop running: `LIVE-level BEFORE/AFTER` only means something if the live drawer is moving under its own power while we write next to it (item 37); (3) the honest live test is a CLOSED day - tomorrow morning the whole Friday tape is folded, has 9+ minutes of future behind every cycle, and is the first night our `VERDICTS MEAN SOMETHING` line can answer without abstaining. What IS worth doing tonight, with the loop untouched, is the live-shaped sail: `--date 2026-11-13 --window 14:00-20:15 --every 180 --prewarm 150 --max-minutes 375` - their 11-13 tape starts at 11:07Z, so 150 minutes of prewarm covers the 60-candle law by ~14:07Z and the day feeds ~100 cycles of real, still-warm market data. The arithmetic to remember: **first fed cycle = tape's first row + 60 candles x stride**, which is why an open day with a late first row cannot be warmed by any cap at all (item 31/40).
44. **A HALF-FIX SHIPPED THREE TIMES BECAUSE MY TESTS GRADED THE WRAPPER, NOT THE NUMBER (item 38's true state, found 2026-11-13 21:40 while reading the owner's receipt instead of my memory):** for two versions I wrote that the look-ahead "fixed" `fwd9=None`. The fix WAS in the file - `win_end += look_min`, `cells_end` in the window dict, `lookahead_min` in the json - and every test I wrote asserted exactly those things: window bounds, `cells_end`, the printed words. None of them asserted the shipped number. The reason the number never moved is one line: `fwd9/pb4 = forward_price(walk.prices, tc, 9)` (`.6:751-752`, `.8:854-855`), and `walk.prices` ends AT `tc` by the causality law - the walk is fed only up to the current cycle **on purpose**, because feeding it further would let a cycle see its own future. So widening the tape read could never reach the scorer. **The law: a fix is not what the code now allows, it is what the output now says. Assert the NUMBER (`fwd9 is not None` and `abs(fwd9) < price`), not the plumbing that makes it possible.** The owner's own data proved the diagnosis in a single line: their `.6` receipt shows `last_row == win_end` (1794486480.0 both) - the tape really was truncated at the cap, so on that rig BOTH causes were live at once.
45. **A RECEIPT IS THE EVIDENCE, NOT MY RECIPE (the v8d packet, and the hole in my own block):** the owner attached 293 honest lines; the first three say `sha256sum: nov13_factory2_code_day8d.patch: No such file or directory`, then `error: can't open patch`, then `book AFTER : 3311 (want 3326)` and `tests in file: 41` with `.6` stamps. v8d was NEVER applied - the sea they sailed was v8b - and **my block carried no abort gate**, so it sailed the wrong tree for six minutes and printed a beautiful, useless paper. Two rules follow, and both are now in the recipe's shape: **(a)** a handover block must `exit 1` on a missing patch, a sha mismatch, or a failed `git apply`, and it must SEARCH (`find` over the repo root AND `~/Downloads`) rather than hope; **(b)** I must read the receipt's own gates before I draw any conclusion from its numbers. What the receipt DID prove stands on its own (their first 177 real cycles, 3 real verdicts, `G2 78/78` and `99/99`, zero `stale`, zero `no-eyes`, the order tape feeding 2.49M/3.39M rows) - and its `0/3 episode(s) went the way the door said` is NOT the scored-silence bug I first called it: that abstain exists since `.7`, and the receipt ran `.6`. **Judging state by `want 5282af4` was my other mistake: this owner never committed my tip - their HEAD is `807ac51` with my files as a dirty tree - so state is judged by Book lines + `.N` stamps + `grep -c '^def test_'`, which is exactly how the non-application became visible.**
46. **A REHEARSAL MUST NOT INHERIT A BRAIN IT DID NOT GROW (item 20's completion):** the quarantined mirror `data/gates_sandbox/` PERSISTS between runs, and the borrowed brain READS it back (`session_levels` is written by `step2_market_analysis.py:6693` and read by its loader), so every rehearsal since v3 quietly inherited the previous rehearsal's prev-day levels - nobody asked it to, and nothing printed it. The accident that exposed it was mine and perfect: my new horizon test **passed on a dirty tree and went red on a clean one** - same tape, same code, two answers, 362 bytes of difference. Now `quarantine_mirror()` MOVES the learned files to `gates_sandbox_prev_<stamp>/` before a run (LAW #13: kept, never deleted) and prints `replay mirror : mirror reset: 2 learned item(s), 305 B set aside...`; `--keep-mirror` may inherit, but only while saying `mirror KEPT on purpose ... scored with someone else's history`. `--mirror-dir` lets a rehearsal keep its own scratch brain. Two traps I walked into while building it, recorded because they are this house's favourite kind: **(a)** I derived the mirror path from `--data-dir` while the quarantine anchors on `config.DATA_DIR`, so a FULL mirror printed `mirror empty` (item 33 with a folder instead of a number); **(b)** I put the set-aside folder INSIDE the folder being moved, `shutil.move` refused with EISDIR, and my `except OSError: pass` SWALLOWED it - so the reset now returns `mirror reset FAILED after n/N: <file>: <error>` on its face. **A silent `except` in a diagnostic is worse than no diagnostic.**
47. **THE LEARNER WAS BEING FED THE FUTURE (found by a test I wrote to catch something else):** `available_days()` returns days NEWEST-FIRST - which is right for "the last N days" - and `main()` looped over that list directly, so `--sea 2` sailed 11-13 BEFORE 11-12 and the second day inherited a level book written by the day AFTER it. On the owner's real night that means some of the `prev_day_*` rungs on 11-12 came from 11-13's prints. `for dstr in sorted(days)` fixes it and the paper now prints `day order : chronological (2026-11-04 -> 2026-11-05); the learner may only inherit the PAST`, with `day_order`/`mirror` in the aggregate json so a receipt can be audited without rerunning anything. **The general law: any time you hand a sequence of days to something that LEARNS as it goes, the order is a LAW, not a detail - and a list that is correct for one purpose (newest-first for "recent") is a bug for another.**
**ENTRY 7 LAWS ADDED (small, cheap, clever — "less work and easier"):**
- **L-A (folded-diary law):** any new diary in this repo writes ~1 KB per heartbeat, gzips finished days, NEVER trims, and its in-RAM state is ring-capped. Disk cost must be provable before a loop is allowed to run overnight.
- **L-B (replay-first law):** a new decision-maker must be sailed on RECORDED RAW TAPES the same night it passes its tests; live days after that prove plumbing only. A verdict that cannot be reproduced from the tape is not a verdict.
- **L-C (stamp-every-assumption law):** when an input is missing, the organ abstains and the diary names the assumption (`eyes:assumed-fresh`, `spread:assumed`, `l3q:assumed`, `news:assumed-quiet`, `risk:neutral(rehearsal)`, `votes:absent(l1)`). The night teacher refuses unknown stamp words (`STAMPS ARE A KNOWN VOCABULARY`) — silence and guessing are the same crime.
- **L-D (design-story law):** every system we design is written HERE with its steps and its receipts, because the next improvement should start from a map, not from memory.
48. **THE MAP IS SHOWN AT THE END OF EVERY MESSAGE until the last step is closed (owner, `2026-11-14`: *"as long as we won't finish the last step in the Factory2-map, after each communication show me again and again the Factory2-map, it's help me to see where we are exactly step by step"*):** the page is not a certificate, it is a **tracker** — it opens with a `YOU ARE HERE` block and `[x] [!] [?] [ ] [-]` marks, and the marker MOVES in the same commit as the work. A map that does not move is a poster. The rule expires by itself the day Room E closes, and the same instinct applies to any long walk: **show the position, not just the plan.**

49. **A CONFIRMATION IS A CHECKLIST, NOT A SENTIMENT (Room A, `2026-11-14`):** when the owner asks "confirm X is finished, it needs nothing else", the answer is the CONTRACT as a table — every line with the receipt that closes it, and any line that cannot be signed named out loud (Room A's determinism line waited on the owner's own rerun, so the room was "one signature away", not closed). Two refusals were also required: known-but-unblocked work (TODO 12/12b) is not "nothing else", it is "not yet, on purpose". The habit paid for itself in the same minute — reading Room A line by line to confirm it found a mislabelled skip word (two freshness laws: `stale` names the sea's 240 s tape rule while the 15 s fire-law sits disarmed by construction in replay) that three graded nights had missed. A checklist finds what a summary cannot. When a check uncovers a defect, RESCOPE the step it belongs to instead of closing the room, and keep the order: measure first, patch second.

50. **A RECEIPT RECORDS THE MACHINE, NOT ONLY THE VERDICT (`2026-11-17`, from the belt1b receipt):** the same paper that closed Room A also carried two facts nobody asked for — the operator's clock reads **2026-11-17**, three days after our session date, visible in `== BELT-1b 2026-11-17T00:41:09Z` and in every `gates_sandbox_prev_20261117T…` name; and the live mirror timestamp `2026-11-17_00:58:04` **unchanged before and after** the patch, the 55 tests and two re-sails. A clock offset changes nothing already measured here (that leg ran with an explicit `--date 2026-11-06`), but it changes any code that derives *today* or *the last N days* from the wall clock — `available_days()` and `day_token` read **names**, not dates. The rule: **stamp `date -u` and the input-set selector's own view into every block**, so a shifted clock or an empty drawer is visible in the receipt instead of silently moving the day list; and when a rehearsal quotes a duration, name the machine (159.17 s there / 44.09 s here for one suite) or an operator will mistake slow for stuck. Corollary, offered not imposed: a keep-forever law (the 69 `gates_sandbox_prev_*` folders) is right per run and wrong as a habit — so every receipt that counts such folders is also a housekeeping receipt, and cleanup stays the owner's call, listing first, deleting never without a word.

51. **A PROBE MUST REPLAY THE WALK, NOT MEASURE MY THEORY (`2026-11-17`, the step-2 probe that disagreed with reality):** v1 predicted `stale=0` for a day the tool recorded as 65, because it counted PRINT-GAPS > 240s — the rule I *believed* was firing — while building its event list from prints only. The tool's list is `(ep, price, size, line)` and depth rows enter it as `(ep, 0, 0, line)`, which move `ev_i` but never `last_ep`; and `grid` runs to `cells_end` regardless of where the tape ends. Both facts make `stale` a label for 'depth before the first print' and 'a frozen tail', and my probe was blind to both by construction. A disagreement between a probe and the thing it probes is a SECOND BUG, not a finding — unless the probe reproduces the subject's own order of operations and data shape. Standing rules: (a) rehearse a probe on a fixture whose answer is computed BY HAND, and make the fixture carry EVERY arm of the rule being tested (mine did: arm B=20, arm A=259 = 94 mid-gap + 165 frozen tail, 301=279+21+1 ✓); (b) when a prediction is falsified, publish the falsification — the dead theory is as valuable as the live one; (c) print the subject's OWN recorded inputs (`src`, `window.first_row/last_row`, `cap_min`) beside the probe's recomputed ones, because a difference there is a different cause with the same number.

**51b. AMENDMENT, SAME LAW, ONE DAY LATER — A PROBE MAY NOT GUESS A DIAL (`2026-11-17`):** the replacement this entry celebrated is ALSO dead. v2 hard-coded `--window 08:00-23:00` to "replay the walk" while the owner's own sailing used `08:00-22:00` (`nov14_belt1_block.sh:45-46`), and it announced an **arm B (check-ORDER) mechanism that measured exactly `0` on every real day** (`arm B = 0`, `first_PRINT == first_ROW` — their papers are `src=tapes:own+mbo`, so rows exist from the first minute). Two lessons, one law: **(i) a probe takes its bounds from the subject's own record** — read the `window:` stamp if the paper has one, otherwise SOLVE for the hour-end whose grid equals the paper's own `cycles + sum(skips)`, and PRINT `INFERRED` when it did that and `ASSUMED` when it fell back; **(ii) a probe must not report a mechanism it never isolated** — v2's arm split tested `last_px > 0`, which is TRUE for a frozen tail, so "arm A = a real silence" was a label applied to 100 % of days without separating anything. My hand arithmetic on the owner's real windows over-predicted by exactly the 20 phantom cells per day that the 23:00 guess added, and that phantom nearly sent me hunting a third rule that does not exist. When a paper has no `skip_kinds`, the only honest line is `NO RECORD`, never `MATCH`.


**52. A WORD THAT COVERS TWO STATES IS A BUG YOU CAN MEASURE, AND A TEST YOU CANNOT BREAK IS NOT A TEST (`2026-11-17`):** the sea had one label for "the feed is late" and "the day is over", so for as long as anyone can remember, a normal end-of-day was booked as a malfunction — and the way it was finally settled was arithmetic on the owner's own numbers, not a new dial. Two habits follow. (a) When one counter answers two different questions, SPLIT THE NAME before touching the threshold, and let the paper itself say which state it was (here: name the tape's last row in the same breath as the tail), so the next reader never needs a probe. (b) Before a test is "green", BREAK the fix and make sure the test flinches — my first draft passed while the fix was disarmed, because `> 0` is not a prediction; assert the number, and derive it from the artifact under test (law 34 again: never memorise what the paper can tell you).
