## 3.5 THE FLIP MEASURED — Code Day 3.9's receipt, read from the owner's own `.txt` (`2026-11-14`)

**What was installed.** `nov14_factory2_code_day8f.patch` (sha256 `84837abf…`, 326 lines, five files: `HOLY_BOOK.md` +5, `committee.py` +62, `test_factory2.py` +141, `tools/gates_replay.py` 6, `tools/gates_report.py` 2) applied clean on the owner's tree at `807ac51`: `sha OK`, `APPLIES-CLEAN`, `book 3331 → 3336`, `defs 48 → 54`, `pytest-exit: 0` (71 passed), stamps `gates.replay.2026-11-14.0` / `gates.report.2026-11-14.0`, `dirty 18` before AND after, and the LIVE mirror untouched at both ends (`2026-11-13 22:24:10`). **Nothing trades; the hall stays `--hall-off`.** `factory2_gates.py` is NOT in the paper (5 files, none of them that one), and the flip arms only on a `REPLAY/` mark inside `cyc["symbol"]`, so the live path is byte-identical.

**The two-day sail (the owner's `data/v8f_sail_20261114.txt`, their own block's output).** 17 verdicts across `2026-11-12` + `2026-11-13` — 6 and 11 — **all 17 with `TOLL=paid`**, and the tally closes: `NO-VERDICT 156 + NO-EDGE 4 + allowed 17 = 177`, exactly the 177 cycles v8e had counted. `6/17 MEASURED` went the way the door said, and the teacher moved `[WARN] VERDICTS MEAN SOMETHING` to `[PASS]` — which is the whole purpose of the law, not a compliment to the signal.

**Why the number went UP, not down (pre-declared, resolved).** The three outcomes were written before the run. We got the third shape: more tickets, because the OLD law was refusing to speak on cycles where the toll was ALREADY paid — a paid toll means the move has happened and a bounce is now the honest call, and the old code silenced exactly those. Friday went 2 → 11. `NO-EDGE=4` is the new refusal firing on 2+2 cycles (2 per day; the day-merge sums `fails`, so the aggregate agrees with the receipt). Not one pre-declared outcome came back as "the law muzzled the hall".

**The fat-day exam (`2026-11-05`, their fullest tape, 10 slices, `data/gates_d05.json` + `data/gates_d05_grade.txt`).** 240 cycles (22 refused: stale 2, warm-up 20), `G2 with a rung: 240 (100%)`, band `2739 of 2739` fight lines inside ±80, **23 verdicts, `8/23` = 34.8 %, `toll paid by 23/23`**, 6 PASS / 0 WARN / 0 FAIL, five rungs (`whale`, `htf_poc`, `onh`, `value_edge`, `ibh`), both PUSH directions, longest hold 147 min, `max closes beyond a rung in one hold: 49`.

**THE FINDING (the sentence the whole detour bought).** Combining them honestly: **`14/40 = 35 %`** of paid-toll verdicts agree that price bounces at the rung, at +9 minutes, on gold, on the owner's own tapes — about 15 points BELOW a coin flip, i.e. the tape keeps going the way it was already going. The number is no longer explainable as first-touch noise, because **every one of the 40 tickets carries `TOLL=paid`**. For contrast, v8e before this law printed **0 of 10**.

**Pre-registration kept in full, including the miss.** I wrote my expectation down before the run: `25–60` verdicts, all paid, `30–45 %` agree. Result: `23` verdicts = **MISS, low**; `34.8 %` = hit; `23/23` paid = hit. Recorded because a prediction that is only quoted when it lands is not a prediction.

**A side effect worth naming: borrowed memory moves scores.** Thursday's two shared episodes changed (`12:06` −2.4 → −3.6, `12:24` −7.2 → −3.6) purely because sailing `11-12` and `11-13` together in chronological order lets `11-12` inherit `11-11`'s levels. Same code, same tape, different day-order → different numbers. Any future comparison must hold the day set fixed.

**What is NOT established (do not let this section be read as saying these).** (a) A win count is not an edge — 16/23 wins of +0.4 USD is worse than 9/23 wins of +6 USD, and **USD per verdict has never been measured**; (b) `35 %` rests on two adjacent days plus one fat day chosen for its size, **not** on out-of-sample tape (`2026-11-04` = 7 slices, `2026-11-06` = 6 slices have never been touched); (c) no rule has been reversed — the hall still refuses to open on a bounce it has not earned; (d) `git status --porcelain` showing `??` for `factory2_gates.py` proves only UNTRACKED, not "unchanged since `807ac51`".

**The next measurement, and why it is not another percentage.** New read-only tool `v8f_payoff.py` (sha256 `80ed3828…`, 5,343 bytes, no production line touched): signs each episode by the teacher's OWN law (`gates_report.py:_way` — BOUNCE wants a rise, BREAK wants a fall), then prints mean/median USD per verdict for the call as made AND as flipped, split by push / rung / toll / horizon. Tested against a fixture built from the owner's pasted 11-05 numbers, and cross-checked so its `win n/N` must equal the teacher's own count — if the two disagree, the disagreement is the bug report, not a number to shop. Cost reality stated as arithmetic, not law: GC is 100 oz and a 0.10 tick is $10 per contract, so ~$2/oz round trip must be beaten before "edge" is a word we may use.

**My own errors this round, same section, because that is what this section is for.** (1) `grep -o "NO-EDGE=[0-9]*"` — the json says `"NO-EDGE": 4`, no `=`; (2) a `sed 's/}, {/…/'` pipeline that matched nothing, because the sea json prints one field per line; (3) `head`/`tail` on a receipt fed me the OLDEST globbed artifact (a Wednesday v8b file, `fwd9 None`) — and `ls -l data/gates_sea*.json | tail -1`, the line at **my own block's line 148**, still sorts alphabetically, the exact trap written as item 49; (4) I printed a spread/slippage figure as if it came from the Book when it came from me. Every grep handed to the owner must now be run against a file whose format I have just re-read in code, and any glob in operator paper sorts by mtime with the path echoed.


### 3.5b THE MONEY, MEASURED — first payoff number of factory 2 (`2026-11-14`, owner's `data/v8f_payoff_d05.txt`)

**The number.** On `2026-11-05`, 23 paid-toll verdicts, all `BOUNCE`, `fwd9` in USD/oz: **the door's own calls lose -0.89/oz = -89 USD per contract**, gross. Fading them (doing the opposite) wins **+0.89/oz = +89 gross, +0.69 = +69 net of a 2-tick round trip**. `sd 3.92`, **t = +0.85**, 95 % CI of the flip `[-1.00, +2.38]`/oz, `right 15/23 gross / 14/23 net`. Verdict: **the sign is interesting, the size is nothing.** A t under 2 on 23 tickets from one day is not an edge and must never be described as one.

**What the 23 rows actually look like (the fragility, in one glance).** Every winner is small (+0.10, +0.50, +0.90, +1.50, +2.00, +3.60, +4.90) and one row carries the whole loss side (-7.10, -7.70, -8.70, -6.70, -2.80). So the "flip wins" statement is arithmetic on a coin-flip distribution, not a mechanism: 15 small correct answers and a handful of large wrong ones cancel to +0.89.

**The market fact under it: this tape CONTINUES, it does not revert.** Raw 9-minute drift after these verdicts: `push=UP` n=8 mean **-1.75/oz (-175/contract)**, `push=DOWN` n=15 mean **-0.43/oz (-43/contract)**. Both negative, both the wrong side of a bounce call. The average size of a move at a tested rung is **2.75/oz = 275/contract** (mean |fwd9|) - that is a VOLATILITY figure, useful for sizing and for costing, and it is NOT a P&L. The one legitimate use: a long straddle at these levels is paid 275/contract per 9 minutes, before premium.

**Three versions of one formula, two of them wrong - recorded because both errors looked like results.**
1. **v1** returned raw `fwd9` under the heading `fwd9, signed`. For an all-BOUNCE tape a signed buy ticket *is* the drift, so the **arithmetic was right and the label was wrong**. The danger of a right answer with a wrong derivation: nobody checks the next one.
2. **v2** tried to "fix" it with `abs(f)` re-negated on a loss: `0.0 - abs(-1.2)` is `-1.2`, i.e. **f again - a sign written twice is a sign cancelled.** The branch was a no-op and I did not notice for two rounds because I kept confirming the label instead of the number.
3. **v3** paid a ticket `+|f|` when right and `-|f|` when wrong, then *reflected the loss back as a profit*. Mean **+2.75/oz with t +4.60, 95 % CI entirely above zero** - the most convincing number produced in this entire project, and pure fiction: no directional trade is paid for being wrong by the size of its own mistake. It is a **straddle payout with the premium never subtracted**. Had this been shipped, a volatility belief would have entered the Book as a directional edge.
**LAW (standing):** a payoff must be written as `drift x the side the door actually took` and then **cross-checked against the teacher's own count at zero cost** - `right n/N` at `--friction 0` must equal `VERDICTS MEAN SOMETHING n/N` in the report. That test is in the tool and is the reason v3 died.
**Also standing:** gross and net counts are printed separately (`right 8/23 gross, 7/23 net`). Friction moving a ticket across zero is information, not a detail to fold away.

**My operator errors in the same hour, because the Book is where I write them:** two broken greps handed over (`^ +2026-` matched only after I had already printed a pattern that could not match a three-space indent; `"NO-EDGE=[0-9]*"` vs the json's `"NO-EDGE": 4`), a `sed` on a field order I had not re-read, and a "GC tick = $2/oz round trip" line I printed as if it came from the Book when it was my own arithmetic (correct figure: 1 tick = $0.10/oz = $10/contract; ~$20/contract for 2 ticks). I also misread `-1.2 * -1 = 1.2` and `1.2 * -1 = -1.2` as an interpreter fault and spent three tool calls suspecting the sandbox before suspecting my transcription. **No arithmetic in this project gets reported from a remembered expression again - only from a file that was just printed and just executed.**

**Not established, after this section:** out-of-sample days (`2026-11-04`, `2026-11-06`) still unsailed; the flip's `t=0.85` on n=23 from one day; no commission/slot/slippage model from the owner's broker; the hall still `--hall-off`; nothing trades. `pnl()` refuses to price `BREAK` tickets on principle - this tape has none, and if a day ever has them they must be counted, not mirrored by a formula I have already broken twice.


### 3.5c OUT OF SAMPLE: the door is RELIABLY WRONG and that is still not money (`2026-11-14`, owner's own sail of `2026-11-04` + `2026-11-06`, never looked at before)

**What was promised before the run.** "13-15 more paid tickets in the same direction moves `t` toward 2 and we have a measurement; if they scatter, gold-rung direction is finished." They scattered. The result is the second branch and this section closes the question.

**The two new days, sailed once, out of sample.** `2026-11-04`: 208 cycles, **22 verdicts**, AS SAID mean **+0.14/oz**, right 8/22 (36.4 %), sd 4.85, t +0.13; raw drift after push=UP **+1.10**, after push=DOWN **-0.83**. `2026-11-06`: 124 cycles, **8 verdicts**, mean **+0.91/oz**, right 5/8 (62.5 %), sd 5.60, t +0.46; drift after UP +0.28, after DOWN **+1.97**. Both `HEALTH: PASS`, `TOLL=paid` on the shown tickets, 3,336,284 and 3,178,771 ticks-tape `Mbo` rows ignored as fake-id (item 25 behaving exactly as designed).

**POOLED, all three days, one number:** `n=53` paid-toll verdicts, **right 21/53 = 39.6 %**, mean **-0.19/oz = -19 USD/contract**, pooled sd 4.55, **t = -0.31**, 95 % CI `[-1.45, +1.07]`/oz. Flipped: +0.19/oz = +19 gross, **-0.9/contract net of a 2-tick round trip, -20.9 net of 4 ticks**. To reach t=2 at n=53 this needed |mean| >= 1.25/oz = 125/contract. It has 0.19.

**THE SENTENCE (the one this whole arc bought), stated at exactly its own width:** *On the owner's GC raw tapes, at a tested rung, measured over +9 MINUTES, NEITHER direction pays - not the bounce, not the fade, not the continuation.* 53 tickets, three separate days, out-of-sample days included. **The sentence is about the 9-minute horizon and nothing else.** It must NOT be widened by a reader: the only +12-minute number in evidence is day `2026-11-05` alone (`pb4` mean -0.77/oz, right 9/23, t -0.84), and the +12 min figure for 04/06 was never printed - so no horizon-ladder verdict exists yet in either direction. Saying "the tape is dead" from this table would be the same crime as saying "the tape is gold": one horizon, extrapolated.

**THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE LINE IN THE CHAPTER.** Pooled, the *count* is significant: 21/53 right gives an exact two-sided sign test **p = 0.001** - far beyond the 0.21 that 8/23 alone produced. So the door is **reliably, statistically anti-predictive**: it is worse than a coin at calling direction, and that fact is real, reproducible out of sample, and worth roughly **minus nine dollars per contract** after two ticks of friction. The mechanism of the disappearance: it loses *small* when wrong-by-a-hair and wins *small* when right; the days swing -89 / +14 / +91 USD/contract, so the per-day mean IS the noise. **A significant direction test and a dead payoff can both be true at once. Any future bar in this Book must carry a payoff column, never a win rate alone** (this is item 50's rule, now with numbers to prove why it exists).

**Continuation is not the rescue either.** The drift-sign story that looked promising on 11-05 (both push buckets negative) fails out of sample: DOWN-push days were negative twice of three, UP-push positive once of three. No stable law in either direction at 9 minutes.

**Open question, deliberately left open (do not read 3.5c as "the tape is dead"):** everything above is measured at ONE horizon, +9 min, chosen for plumbing reasons (the 15-minute look-ahead limit of v8e, item 46). The auction tradition in Entry 1 expects value to be re-tested over 30-90 minutes, so the honest next bar is a **HORIZON LADDER** on the same tickets - `fwd9` vs `pb4` (+12 min, already in every episode record) and further out - across all three days, pooled, with payoff and t, never a count alone. Measurement-only; no gate, no hall, no trade. If the ladder is flat, the factory keeps its plumbing and the aim moves to day-state (balance vs imbalance) instead of rung direction.

**Errors recorded with the result.** The row-dump `grep -E "^   [0-9]{4}-"` I handed over produced **30** lines where the three days hold **53** episodes - another of my untested shell patterns (and the third this week); pooled arithmetic was therefore done from the per-day `mean`/`sd`/`n` the tool printed, which is exact for the mean and only approximate for the pooled sd (I added the between-day shift term; with n=23/22/8 and means that differ by ~1/oz the approximation cannot move `t` from 0.3 toward 2). Any future per-row pooling runs through python, not grep.


### 3.5d SCOPE, SET BY THE OWNER (`2026-11-14, standing): prove the FACTORY, not the signal

**The instruction, in the owner's words:** *"now we are trying to find out if our new Factory2 is working end to end and it's fine. later we make sure there is no problem with the work, we steps one by one each part and do manager check and fix everything, for now we just check the process and how it act in general."*

**What that changes, immediately.** The horizon-ladder idea (v8f.1, `look_min` + 30/60/90-min columns + a per-episode `eyes` field) is **PARKED, not rejected** - it is signal work, and signal work is out of season. Options A/B/C handed over the same day are withdrawn and replaced by one thing: a part-by-part process inspection of the factory. The 3.5/3.5b/3.5c numbers stay exactly where they are - they are *evidence the plumbing works*, which is now the whole use of them.

**What "end to end, working" must mean here, so it cannot be quietly redefined later:** raw tape from days ago → cycles → gates → a card per verdict → sea json → teacher report → a receipt the owner can read, with (a) every cycle accounted for, (b) every refusal named, (c) every stamp inside a known vocabulary, (d) live files byte-identical, (e) the same input producing the same output. **Not** "it makes money". Money is a later chapter with its own bar (item 50: no win rate without a payoff column).

**The part list to be inspected, one at a time, each with its own manager check.** BELT (cycle bookkeeping: `_skip()` kinds `no-eyes/stale/warm-up/brain/snapshot`, the sum must equal the cycle count) · EYES (freshness/staleness rule) · DOORMAN (G1/G2/G3 and the v8f flip: `NO-VERDICT + NO-EDGE + allowed = total`, closed on the owner's receipts at 156+4+17=177 and on 240/22/23) · MAP (rung band: `2739 of 2739` lines inside ±80 on 11-05) · L3/ORDER FEED (order rows consumed, ticks-tape `Mbo` rows counted as fake-id and ignored: 5,359,249 / 3,336,284 / 3,178,771) · LEDGER (committee defs 54, Book 3336) · REPORT (11 gates, vocabulary check `STAMPS_ALLOWED` = 11 words at `gates_report.py:42-56`, unknown stamp ⇒ WARN at `:167`) · MIRROR (quarantine, live untouched: `2026-11-13 22:24:10` before and after) · BUDGET (the owner's own caps: `CPU_PCT_BUDGET = 0.3` and `MAX_LINE_BYTES = 1100`, at `gates_report.py:40-41` - never yet measured on a full-fat day) · DETERMINISM (same day twice, byte-identical json) · RECEIPT (block exit, sha, `APPLIES-CLEAN`, every number read from the file, never remembered).

**Verified before writing, not remembered:** the counts and stamps above come from the owner's own receipts and from `git show 99b3cc8:tools/gates_report.py` (`:39` `REPORT_VERSION` pre-v8f, `:40-41` the two budget caps, `:42` the stamp vocabulary, `:132/:167` the unknown-stamp gate) and `:846/:870/:873/:894/:926/:937` of `tools/gates_replay.py` (the five skip kinds). **One item is knowingly unproven and stays open:** a same-day rerun has never been byte-compared, so determinism is asserted nowhere and must not be claimed.


### 3.5e LAWS #14 — NO FILE WITHOUT A HOME (`2026-11-14`, owner's standing instruction, verbatim)

*"if we can do the updates and upgrades and follow our process with the patch file, that will be the best; if not, then we are adding more files to our bookmap folder, we love the keep this app light and organized well."*

**The law.** A change goes into an EXISTING file of the factory by PATCH. A new file is a last resort and must state the file it could not live in and why. The folder is the product: every extra `tools/x.py` is one more thing the owner must recognise, keep, and not delete.

**Born from a live breach by me, same day, caught before it shipped.** BELT-1 (the factory-counts-itself check) was about to arrive as TWO new files - `tools/v8f_belt_check.py` (88 lines) and `tools/v8f_belt_block.sh` (57 lines) - both already committed shadow-side at `399d8b7`. Both are REJECTED by this law and folded:
- the three counter assertions (`errors == sum(skips)`, `verdicts == len(episodes)`, `g2_pass <= cycles`) become **one gate in `tools/gates_report.py`**, using machinery that already exists there: the teacher already reads the sea json and already has a grading table (`T.add(grade, name, headline, why)` at `:70-79`, sea-based gates at `:206/:211/:216/:440`). An audit that belongs to the Teacher lives with the Teacher;
- the determinism leg (sail one day twice, compare bytes) becomes **one test in `test_factory2.py`**, skipped with a named reason when the tape is absent - tests are the only place a repeat check has ever belonged;
- `tools/v8f_belt_block.sh` dies entirely: the operator block is pasted, never stored in the repo (it is paper, not product).
**Net new files after this section: ZERO. Files changed: `HOLY_BOOK.md`, `tools/gates_report.py`, `test_factory2.py`.**

**Standing consequence for every future handover:** ONE patch + ONE pasteable block, and the patch may add a file only when the check has no home - e.g. a checker with no report to attach to. If a handover ever adds a file, the receipt must contain the line `NEW FILE JUSTIFIED: <file> because <no-home reason>`, and the Book entry must repeat it. A receipt without that line, adding a file, is a law violation and gets withdrawn.

**Why this is a MEASUREMENT law and not tidiness:** the folder is where the owner's trust lives. Two stray `v8f_*` files would have been the first permanent litter of the shadow era - and the day a stranger (or the owner, six months out) reads `tools/`, every file must be either factory or test. Nothing else is allowed to look official.


### 3.5f THE BOOK IS AT THE ROOT, AND IT IS A WORKING SURFACE (`2026-11-14`, two owner catches, same message)

**Catch 1, a path I invented.** My BELT-1 operator block printed, twice, `bash: data/HOLY_BOOK.md: No such file or directory`. Truth from git: `git ls-tree -r --name-only 99b3cc8 | grep -i holy_book` -> **`HOLY_BOOK.md`**, one file, repo root, nothing under `data/`. So the two `book=` fields in my own receipt were EMPTY, not zero. My shipped v8f recipe (`v8f_sail_recipe.txt:127/:191/:205/:220`) used the correct root path and even ABORTS if the Book did not move to 3336 - meaning this was not a systemic confusion but a fresh one: **I stopped reading the path from git the moment I wrote a new block.** Fixed as hygiene law 10. The empty lines are also why the block is written to fail loudly: `wc -l <` on a missing file is an error on the terminal, and the owner saw it immediately.

**Catch 2, a standing instruction.** *"We should keep checking our holy book and keep note and update it as well."* Recorded as hygiene law 11: read the open sections at the START of a code day (STEP 2g / STRATEGY ledger / 3.5a-f are live), write the receipt at the END, and every rule that changes HOW we work becomes a LAW in the Book, not a chat message. Practical consequence for the owner: the process to-do list now exists in TWO places by design - `FACTORY2_TODO.md` in the workspace (what to run next) and the Book (why each step exists and what it already proved) - and a step may not be called closed until the Book carries its receipt.

**Also settled from the same run:** the block was NOT stuck. `python -m pytest -q test_factory2.py` runs 55 tests, several of which spawn real replays; on my box the same suite took **47 s** (`55 passed in 46.86s`) and theirs may take a few minutes because the determinism leg reads real tape folders. A block that writes its receipt line by line (`say ... >> "$R"`) is the answer to "is it doing anything?" - and the continuation route (run the remaining legs, reuse the already-written receipt) was used instead of restarting, so no leg ran twice and the patch was never re-applied.


### 3.5g STEP 1 RESULT: the belt adds up, the RERUN did not — and the difference was my own paper (`2026-11-14`, from the owner's `data/belt1_20261114.txt`)

**What their receipt said.** `sha OK` (patch `e26be7523b4735fa…`, 8,649 B), `APPLIES-CLEAN`, 2 files touched, no new file. The new gate ran on three real days and the tally came back clean — on `2026-11-06`: `errors == sum(skips)` **84 vs 84** (`stale:65 warm-up:19`), `verdicts == len(episodes)` **8 vs 8**, `tickets <= cycles` **8 vs 124**, `NIGHT GRADE: 7 PASS / 0 WARN / 0 FAIL`, verdict line `belt FAIL lines=0`. **So: every minute the sea refused was refused for a named reason, on every day graded.** That closes the BELT half of step 1 (question 1) and, with `stale:65` named rather than lumped, it is the first time that day's 84 refusals were ever itemised.

**And the leg nobody had ever run said `DIFFERENT`.** Same day, same flags, sailed twice: `run1 exit 0  run2 exit 0  cmp exit 1`, two different sha256s, both logs printing `8 verdict(s)`. `determinism=DIFFERENT` in the verdict line. **This is why the leg existed.** Had I only run the belt (which passed) and called step 1 closed, the factory would still have carried an untested assumption under every number in this Book.

**The diagnosis, measured not guessed.** Reproduced independently on my side at the owner's exact tree state (`99b3cc8` + v8f + belt1) with a synthetic sail: **`DIFFERING FIELDS: ['mirror']` — one field out of forty; `episodes` and `_days` byte-equal; `cycles`/`g2_pass`/`fwd_rows`/`printed_by` all SAME.** Cause: `quarantine_mirror()` renames the old live mirror on every run and returns prose naming that folder (`gates_replay.py:1383` put the string into `agg["mirror"]`), so the second sail of a day can never print the same json as the first. The "5 earlier rehearsal(s) waiting beside it" counter and the `.N` suffix made it worse. **The measurement was deterministic; the paperwork was not.**

**The fix (`belt1b`, sha256 `95c0b92c3950919d…`, 3,752 B, 61 lines, 3 files, still ZERO new files per LAWS #14).** `tools/gates_replay.py`: the json gets a normalised `mirror` field (`gates_sandbox_prev_<RUN>`, rehearsal count stripped); the raw prose is printed to the log, because the operator must still see WHICH folder holds yesterday's mirror. Stamp `.0 → .1`, with the pin in `test_factory2.py:1807` and the advice string in `gates_report.py:514` moved in the same breath (seal-moves-with-code). Rehearsed end to end on a fresh worktree: v8f → belt1 → belt1b `--check` = **APPLIES-CLEAN**, applied, **`55 passed in 44.09 s`**, and the previously failing leg now returns **`run1=0 run2=0 cmp=0` IDENTICAL** while both logs still print their distinct folder names.

**LAW (standing, hygiene #12):** *a machine-readable record must contain no run-volatile prose.* Timestamps, temp-dir names, counters that grow per run and retry numbers belong in the LOG; the paper an audit compares must be a function of the INPUT only. The test that enforces it is the cheapest one we own — sail the same day twice and `cmp` the json — so it can never rot, and a "PASS" from it means bytes, not vibes.

### 3.5i ROOM A CONFIRMED — and the confirmation re-opened step 2 (`2026-11-14`, owner: *"confirm our room A is finished, it doesn't need anything else to work for it"*)

The owner asked for a confirmation, not a comfort. **Room A is DONE for the job it was given — 5 of its 6 contract
lines carry receipts; the 6th waits on ONE run that is in the owner's hand, so the room is *one signature away*,
not closed.** Met and evidenced: every cycle accounted for (`errors == sum(skips)` 84 vs 84, `verdicts ==
len(episodes)` 8 vs 8, `tickets <= cycles` 8 vs 124, `belt FAIL lines=0` on 3 days) · every refusal named in a
CLOSED five-word vocabulary, an unknown word breaking the equality on purpose (proved on four fixtures incl.
`ghost-cause`) · the live house untouched (quarantine copy, original only stat'ed, `2026-11-13 22:24:10` in every
receipt) · the parser SHARED rather than copied (`gates_replay.py:765-790` → `bbp._BridgeTail.__new__` →
`tail._handle_line` at `:881`; the old factory's own `raw_replay.py:2186` and `backtest.py:11` do the identical
thing, so "copy the exact work" was already true as an IMPORT) · law N1 held (nothing in the old factory edited).
Not signed: **same input → same output** — Q2 caught `mirror` prose, `belt1b` is rehearsed and applied-clean on my
side, but the owner's rerun is the receipt and it has not come back. And **"needs nothing else" is refused for two
items**: TODO 12 (`--from-raw-tail`, live entering at the top of Room B instead of through Room A) and TODO 12b
(our file-discovery is a re-typed glob at `:67-116` while the old factory owns `_discover_raw_files:165` /
`discover_available_days:271` / `_iter_filtered_csv_events:511`) — both recorded as *deliberate, unblocked work*,
not as a clean bill of health.

**THE FINDING THE CONFIRMATION PRODUCED — a room with TWO freshness laws, one of them mislabelled.**
`FRESH_MAX_SECONDS = 15.0` (`committee.py:35`) is read at exactly ONE place (`:364`:
`if not (age >= 0 and age <= FRESH_MAX_SECONDS): fails.append("FRESH-EYES")`), and `age_s` comes from
`factory2_gates.py:217-221` as `now_ts - cv["ts"]`. On the tape path **both are the same number** —
`cv["ts"] = float(tc)` (`gates_replay.py:972`, whose own comment says *"eyes age = 0, causal"*) and
`now_ts=float(tc)` (`:975`) — so **fire-law 1 cannot fire in replay, ever**; its `18.6s ages seen` receipt in the
comment belongs to the HALL path. What the diary's `stale` actually counts is a DIFFERENT rule: a bare literal at
`gates_replay.py:872` (`if last_ep < tc - 240.0 or last_px <= 0.0: _skip("stale")`), i.e. *"the tape has printed
nothing for >240 s"* — with **no named constant, no flag, and no sentence in the report** (`gates_report.py:393-395`
prints whatever words the sea wrote). **No number was wrong; the WORD invited a wrong conclusion** — the purest
kind of finding this week produced, and it is law 12's subject ("a field must not mean two things"). The
standing rule that came out of it: **when a skip reason shares its name with a fire-law, rename one of them, and
give every threshold a word that can be grepped.**

**ORDER KEPT: measure, then fix.** Step 2 therefore starts with a READ-ONLY probe (`nov14_step2_block.sh` +
`nov14_eyes_probe.py`, no patch, no repo change but one `data/eyes_20261114.txt`), which re-reads the tape with
the sea's OWN public functions so it can disagree with the tool only where it is wrong. Rehearsed on a fixture I
built with `discover_tape`'s own naming (my first fixture sat in `archive/<day>/` and returned `rows=0` — the
rehearsal caught MY bug before it could "disprove" the owner's data): `prints=1000 gaps>240s=2 max=1202s
PREDICT stale=7 | tool actual=7 MATCH`, and the negative case prints `NO TAPE for a day the papers name`, never a
silent zero. The patch after it: rename the skip word, name the constant `TAPE_PRINT_GAP_SECONDS`, one sentence in
the report — **and the 15-second law must be STATED as unmeasured in replay, not quietly treated as passed.**

### 3.5k STEP 2 MEASURED — MY THEORY DIED, AND THE AUTOPSY FOUND A CHECK-ORDER BUG (`2026-11-17`)

The owner ran `nov14_step2_block.sh` and its receipt (`data/eyes_20261114.txt`, 19 day-papers read, 9 days
walked) falsified me cleanly: **`PREDICT stale=0 | tool actual=65`** on 2026-11-06, with **`max_print_gap=56s`**
there, 31 s on 11-04, 33 s on 11-12 — no day came close to the 240 s of silence my theory needed. Three days
printed MATCH and all three were days where the tool had recorded **`stale: 0`**, so even the agreement was
trivial. The receipt also carried a gift I had not asked for: **11-06's first print is at 11:36Z with `lag=216
min`**, i.e. the tape was already pouring depth rows hours before any trade printed.

Reading the walk after the falsification (`gates_replay.py:864-873`) explains it without touching any threshold:
`no-eyes` is `if ev_i == 0` — **not one ROW yet** — while `stale` is `last_ep < tc - 240.0 or last_px <= 0.0`,
and depth rows enter `events` as `(ep, 0.0, 0.0, line)`, advancing `ev_i` but never `last_ep`. So "depth
arrived, no print yet" skips the honest class (`no-eyes`) and lands on the borrowed word (`stale`). The second
half is arithmetic: `grid` is built from `ceil(events[0][0]/every)` to `_cells_end` whatever the tape's last row
is, so every cycle **after the tape ends** has a frozen `last_ep` and is `stale` too — a **frozen tail**.
The fix is therefore **two new words** (`pre-first-print`, `post-last-row`) for one borrowed one, not the 240 and
not the 15. The doorman's `FRESH_MAX_SECONDS = 15.0` stays exactly as found: inert in replay by construction,
live-measurable on the hall path.

**CORRECTION TO THIS SECTION, WRITTEN THE SAME DAY IT WAS POSTED (law 51b).** Two claims above did not survive
the owner's own `step2b` receipt (`data/eyes2_20261114.txt`, 8 days walked, `arm B = 0` on every one):
- **"the fix is the order of the checks"** — FALSE as a cause. The `ev_i == 0` ordering issue is real in the code,
  but it is **inert on every day the owner has**: their papers are `src=tapes:own+mbo`, so `first_PRINT ==
  first_ROW` and `no-eyes` never mis-fires. The word `pre-first-print` is therefore kept as a NAMED ARM for a
  shape their own tapes have never shown (and 11-13, stamped `cells_end=16:00`, shows `0 cycles after last_row`),
  not as a bug being repaired. Only the **frozen tail** is what `stale` has been hiding.
- **`cells_end (23:00Z)`** — MY DIAL, NOT THEIRS. They sailed `--window 08:00-22:00`. Recomputed at their window
  the frozen tail reproduces the four papers that recorded `stale` **to the unit: 27 / 2 / 65 / 72** (11-04 /
  11-05 / 11-06 / 11-12). At 23:00 every day gained ~20 cells, which is how a phantom "+20 on all four days"
  appeared and nearly became a third rule.
The sentence above now reads: `stale` = **the grid walking past the tape's last row**, `floor((cells_end −
(last_row + 240))/180) + 1`, with the day's own `last_row` named in the paper (that is what the patch adds).

**Method law, the honest part (law 51):** my v1 probe measured my theory, not the tool — its event list had
prints only, so the very rows that cause the effect were invisible to it. v2 (`nov14_eyes2_probe.py`, 200 L,
sha `1f575d369450…`) **replays the walk**: same check order, same row shape, predicting all four classes and
splitting `stale` into arm A (real silence) and arm B (no print yet). Rehearsed on a fixture rebuilt twice — the
first version was wrong (its depth-only stretch sat BEFORE the window, so the grid never saw it, and the
rehearsal caught that too) — the second matches hand arithmetic exactly: `arm B=20`, `arm A=259` (94 mid-gap +
165 frozen tail), `grid=301`, `fed=21`, 301 = 279 + 21 + 1 ✓.


### 3.5l STEP 2 CLOSED AS AN INVESTIGATION — THE ANSWER IS THE OWNER'S OWN ARITHMETIC (`2026-11-17`, Code Day 10)

**The finding, in one line:** `stale` was never lateness. It is **the grid walking past the tape's last row** — a
day whose file ends at 18:43Z inside a window that runs to 22:00Z has 65 cells with nothing to look at, and the
only true sentence about them is "out of data", not "the feed is stale". Proved on their numbers, not mine:
**11-04 `27`, 11-05 `2`, 11-06 `65`, 11-12 `72`** — identical to the four papers that recorded `stale`, with max
print-gaps of 31 / 32 / 56 / 33 s against a 240 s rule. **No threshold is wrong; no market silence happened.**
11-09 is the one day a REAL print-silence exists (`max_print_gap=316s`, one gap > 240), which is why the split
keeps two arms instead of renaming one word.

**What the patch does (3 existing files, 0 new, `11dd056f1f90880d`, 250 L / 16,534 B):**
- `tools/gates_replay.py` — `REPLAY_VERSION` → `gates.replay.2026-11-17.2`; the `240.0` literal gets a name,
  `TAPE_PRINT_GAP_SECONDS`; the one `stale` word becomes `PRE_FIRST_PRINT = "pre-first-print"` (rows, no print
  yet) and `POST_LAST_ROW = "post-last-row"` (a print existed, then the tape ended); both counters exist in all
  three skip dicts; the day-math paper line now prints `tape last row HH:MMZ` and, when a tail is non-zero,
  `tail past the tape: N cell(s) refused for OUT OF DATA, not for a quiet market`.
- `tools/gates_report.py` — `gates.report.2026-11-17.2`; the teacher counts the two new words. **`stale` stays in
  the KNOWN vocabulary on purpose**: dropping it would have turned every honest pre-split paper into a violation,
  which is exactly what my own first cut did and their suite caught (`_kinds` / `_extra` at `:506/:520`). A NEW
  paper carrying `stale` is a FAIL ("the sea is newer than the split and still using the old word"); an OLD one is
  a WARN ("re-sail to learn which of the two causes it was hiding").
- `test_factory2.py` — the seal pins move to `.2` in the same edit, and the cold-open family gains two scenarios:
  (2b) a busy tape ending mid-window reports `post-last-row` EXACTLY equal to the tail derived from the paper's
  own `window` (not a hard-coded number, not a `> 0` shrug), no `stale` anywhere, and `sum(counters) == errors`;
  (2c) a depth-only stretch before the first print is named `pre-first-print`.

**Receipts (this box, `HP` sandbox, `date -u` at run time):** `55 passed in 47.80s` after the patch (was
`54 + 1`); the patch applied to a clean tree round-trips **byte-identical** to the tree I tested; the patched sea
on a fixture whose answer I computed BY HAND prints `post-last-row: 65` where the truth is 65, `warm-up: 20`,
`errors 85 = 65+20`, `grid 241 = cycles 156 + errors 85`, and the paper line reads
`window 08:00-22:00Z, tape first row 10:00Z, tape last row 18:43Z, grid 241 cell(s) | tail past the tape: 65`;
the teacher's counted line reads `no-eyes:0 stale:0 pre-first-print:0 post-last-row:65 warm-up:20 brain:0
snapshot:0`; both guard branches were driven on purpose (old stamp → `[WARN]` exit 0, new stamp → `[FAIL]` exit 1).
**Negation-tested:** disarming either arm makes the new test fail — the FIRST version of (2b) did not, because it
asserted `> 0`, and I only found that by breaking my own fix and watching nothing complain.

**What this does NOT buy:** nothing about the market changed, no threshold moved, no verdict was re-scored, the
hall stays `--hall-off`, the old factory is untouched (law N1), and the owner's live loop was never referenced.
The handover is `nov17_eyes2d_block.sh` (117 L / 8,040 B, sha `a5dafb3eae5f0bd6`), which applies the patch, re-sails
2026-11-06 with THEIR OWN flags, and asks whether the two new words add up to the 65 they used to read. Its
measurement leg was rehearsed against real json before shipping, and that rehearsal found two bugs in my reader —
the per-day file names its counters `skips` while the aggregate names them `skip_kinds`, and the aggregate's
`days` field is a list of day STRINGS, not dicts. A block is only "tested" when its shipped lines have run. The habit this whole step leaves behind is **law 52**.

*Two stamp notes, recorded because a hash that moves must never move silently.* (1) The block's first cut
carried a half-edited expectation line (`want 250/245 lines, sha 60 lines?`) left over from a same-day rewrite,
and my first "fix" of it was a **no-op that `bash -n` blessed** — what caught the lie was that the byte count and
sha did NOT move. (2) The rehearsal run then showed the receipt arriving at ~150 KB, because `--no-diary` prints
one ~1 KB json line per cycle to stdout; the sail now writes `data/eyes2d_sail.log` and quotes only its last 3
lines, so the receipt is **8,128 B / 121 L** on a 156-cycle day. Both repairs are why a handover is judged by
**the file it produces**, never by its exit code, and why the block's identity is stated as measured:
`nov17_eyes2d_block.sh` = **117 L / 8,040 B / sha `a5dafb3eae5f0bd6`** (it moved `126d1240eb96e428` →
`55574eae2fed917b` → this). `bash -n` is not a test; a changed byte count is.

**THE REBUILD RECIPE, CORRECTED (found while re-proving this patch the next day).** My own note for rebuilding the
tree a patch is cut against said `99b3cc8` + `day8f` + `belt1` + `belt1b`; run verbatim it FAILED, because `day8f`
also carries a `HOLY_BOOK.md` leg and the Book in this shadow is not the file that patch expects. The working
recipe is:
`git worktree add .scratch/a 99b3cc8` → `git apply --exclude='HOLY_BOOK.md' nov14_factory2_code_day8f.patch` →
`git apply nov14_factory2_code_day9_belt1.patch` → `git apply nov14_factory2_code_day9_belt1b.patch`.
That tree answers exactly what the owner's disk answers — `gates.replay.2026-11-14.1`, `gates.report.2026-11-14.1`,
`55` test functions — and `nov17_factory2_code_day10_eyes2d.patch` then applies **clean** on top of it. Re-run

`cycles fed : 156 (skipped 85: post-last-row 65, warm-up 20)` with `tail past the tape: 65 cell(s)` — the headline
number, reproduced on a tree rebuilt from scratch, not the cached one. **Corollary for the shelf:** this is why the
three `.patch` files are LOAD-BEARING and stay, while ~98 others were deleted.

**THE DIALS, IN ONE PLACE, NOW THAT THE BLOCK THAT HELD THEM IS GONE** (`nov14_belt1_block.sh:45-46`, deleted
`2026-11-17`): every Factory 2 sail in this project uses

("record the machine") applied to my own tools.

### 3.5n STEP 3 OPENED THE ONLY HONEST WAY — MEASURE FIRST, AND THE MEASURE CAUGHT ME TWICE (`2026-11-17`, one hour after step 2 closed)

Row 3's law is `NO-VERDICT + NO-EDGE + allowed == cycles`, **per day, asserted**. Reading the current code (a tree
rebuilt from `99b3cc8` + `day8f --exclude=HOLY_BOOK.md` + `belt1` + `belt1b` + `eyes2d`, stamps
`gates.replay/.report.2026-11-17.2` = the owner's disk, five commands and no guesswork) shows why it is an
assertion and not a pretty line: `committee.py:398-410` builds `fails` as a **LIST** — after the BOUNCE/BREAK gate
other laws append `FRESH-EYES`, `DECIDE-AT-THE-CLOSE`, so one cycle can carry **two** words — while the sea records
"allowed" only as `tickets` (`gates_replay.py:1048-1049`). Three ways the tally can therefore lie, and all three are
now counted: a multi-word cycle counted twice (`extra word-count > 0`), a refused cycle with **no word at all** (the
quiet lie), and a cycle that is simultaneously allowed and carrying a word.

**So I wrote `nov17_doorman_tally_block.sh` (9,501 B / 167 L / sha `b22eb17a5f36a005`) — READ-ONLY, no re-sail, no
dials** — and step 2's lesson paid for itself immediately. Two bugs, both caught HERE and not on the owner's box:
1. **a diary is not all cycles.** The sea appends one `mode: replay:summary` record per day (it carries `cycles`,
   has no `cycle` and no `final`). My first cut counted it, so a clean fixture reported `refused-without-a-word 1`
   and I was one step from announcing a "quiet lie" that the code never wrote. Fixed: cycle records require BOTH
   `cycle` and `final`; meta lines are counted separately AND the summary's own `cycles` is now a free extra
   assertion (`the summary says cycles=156 -> OK`).
2. **a label that reads like a verdict when it is arithmetic.** `(extra count -1)` next to "multi-word cycles 0"
   looked like a failure. Rewritten as `extra word-count %+d (>0 = a cycle carries 2+ words, <0 = a refusal has NO
   word)` plus a dedicated `the three lies : … 0 | 0 | 0` line, so a PASS is legible as zeros.
After the fixes, the fixture says: `156 cycles = allowed 0 + refused 156`, `NO-VERDICT=156`, `the three lies 0|0|0`,
`diary agrees`, **`== DOORMAN TALLY: PASS ==`**.

**And it was made to flinch (law 52's second half).** Three mutated copies of the same diary, one cycle each:
`fails -> []` → `refused-without-a-word 1` and `BROKEN: unnamed refusals + disagreement`;
`fails -> [NO-VERDICT, FRESH-EYES]` → `multi-word cycles 1`, `extra word-count +1`, `PAPER AND DIARY DISAGREE`;
`allow -> true` (a ticket the paper never counted) → `allowed-but-carries-a-word` + disagreement. Every mutation
changed the verdict; none of them could pass silently. **The scratch trees were then deleted** — the shelf holds
12 items, and the tree is rebuildable in five commands from the four patches that stay for exactly that reason.

**53. READ THE FILE'S SHAPE BEFORE ITS VALUES, AND TEST THE TEST ON MUTATED DATA (`2026-11-17`, the summary line that almost became a finding):** a probe that counts "lines in the diary" counts the sea's own closing record, and a probe that scores a paper's tallies against it will report a lie that no code wrote. Two clauses, both cheap: (a) before any number, print what the file IS — one record per cycle, one summary per day, keys `cycle`/`final` vs `mode`/`cycles` — and use the file's own self-consistency (summary vs count) as an assertion, not a footnote; (b) after the tool says PASS, mutate one record in three directions and require three DIFFERENT verdicts, or the PASS is only the sound of silence. Rehearsal on a hand-computed fixture is what makes (a) and (b) cost one command instead of one owner round-trip.

### 3.5o "IS ROOM A DONE?" — ANSWERED AS A LIST, AND THE LIST CAUGHT MY HEADER (`2026-11-17`)

The owner asked the question that law 49 exists for, and this time the answer had to be turned on my own page
before it was given to them. My map header read **"ROOM A CLOSED 3 of 3"** — wrong twice: the room has **four**
steps (0-3), and the page's own footer still carries `[!] TODO 12 ROOM A LIVE PANE` and `[!] TODO 12b DISCOVERY
PARITY`, i.e. two open rows **wearing this room's name**, plus row 3 (the doorman tally) which measures this
room's tally boy and can still reopen it. A header that overstates the body is how a tracker becomes a poster, so
the header was rewritten in the same commit as this entry, and the ROOM A VERDICT line was moved to sit **after**
step 3 (it had been parked between steps 1 and 2, where it read as "done" to anyone scrolling).

**The answer, as the checklist it is:** the room is CLOSED *for what it was created to prove* — that a minute can
get inside safely and be accounted for — and NOT "finished, needs nothing else". Four steps signed: 0 the two
panes (and today's sail still prints `SEA FED BY: 1 raw-tape day(s)`); 1 the glass case (the live mirror timestamp
`2026-11-17_00:58:04` identical before AND after the patch, the 55 tests and the re-sail, on two independent
receipts now); 2 the eyes/freshness label, closed by the owner's own receipt; 3 the belt and its tally (`84/84`,
`8/8`, `8<=124`, `cmp exit 0`), re-proved today as `grid 208 = 124 + 84` under the new vocabulary. Three things
stay open and are named, not hidden: **TODO 12 / 12b** (the live pane and the discovery parity — deferred on
purpose, `12b` only "after" because re-typing discovery is a design decision, not a bug), **row 3** (measuring
now), and **one number I still cannot reproduce**: `warm-up` 20 in my hand model vs 19 in your paper. It did not
move today, so it is a live question about MY model of the sea, and it is the first thing that can pull Room A
open again if row 3's patch touches the warming law.

**49b. A ROOM IS OPEN WHILE ANY ROW UNDER ITS NAME IS OPEN (`2026-11-17`, my own map header):** "closed" is a
statement about a list, so a header may not outrun its own body — before writing CLOSED, `grep` the page for
`[!]`/`[?]` lines that name the same room, and say what they are. Two corollaries that came out of the same
sixty seconds: put a VERDICT line where the reader reaches it (after the last item it summarises, not in the
middle of them), and prefer "signed for what it set out to prove, with these three things still open" to any
sentence that a future receipt would have to contradict.

### 3.5p ROW 3 MEASURED: THE DIARY WAS HONEST, THE PAPER WAS NOT, AND THE FIX IS CODE (`2026-11-17`, their 02:34Z receipt → my 02:50Z patch)

What the owner ran and what came back — `uploads/doorman_20261117.txt`, 81 L / 10,164 B, `branch=strategy-v2
git=807ac51`, stamps on disk `replay=gates.replay.2026-11-17.2 report=gates.report.2026-11-17.2`, `diaries=6
papers=168`, live mirror `2026-11-17_00:58:04` identical before and after. **On all 1,815 cycle records:**
`allowed+refused == cycles -> OK`, `multi-word cycles 0`, `extra word-count +0`, `the three lies : 0 | 0 | 0`.

| day | cycles | allowed | refused | the words |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11-05 | 542 | 0 | 542 | NO-VERDICT 476, L3Q 61, NO-EDGE 5 |
| 11-06 | 124 | 8 | 116 | NO-VERDICT 112, NO-EDGE 4 |
| 11-12 | 57 | 9 | 48 | NO-VERDICT 48 |
| 11-13 | 177 | 3 | 174 | NO-VERDICT 174 |
| 11-14 | 427 | 17 | 410 | NO-VERDICT 388, L3Q 14, NO-EDGE 8 |
| 11-17 | 488 | 0 | 488 | NO-VERDICT 452, L3Q 28, NO-EDGE 8 |

and **every `gates_*.json` paper printed `cycles N = tickets T + refusal-words N -> DOES NOT ADD UP`.**

**The defect, with its line.** `tools/gates_replay.py:1027-1028` read
`for fl in (fin.get("fails") or ["(allowed)"]):` — a cycle that refused nothing was filed a word anyway, INSIDE
the refusal tally. So `sum(fails) == cycles` on every day and `tickets + sum(fails)` was inflated by exactly the
ticket count (11-06: 124 words for 116 refusals + 8 tickets). `"(allowed)"` occurs nowhere else in the repo
(grep); `gates_report.py:204` already used `or []`. **The diary was right all along** — it stores each cycle's own
list; only the aggregate lied.

**What that does to my promise.** The map told the owner row 3 could close with ZERO code. Corrected here: the
diary side needed no code, but no honest gate could exist on the paper side while the tally invented a refusal.
**Row 3 closes with ONE patch plus one re-sail**, not zero.

**TALLY-2** — `nov17_factory2_code_day11_tally2.patch`, 261 L / 17,698 B / sha256 `4c6247b0227309cd`, 3 files,
stamps → `gates.replay.2026-11-17.3` / `gates.report.2026-11-17.3`:
1. **sea** — the tally counts real words only; a wordless cycle increments `res["allowed"]`; both are written to
   the paper and summed in the cross-day merge; the print becomes `refusal tally : … | allowed N of M cycle(s)`
   and appends `** TALLY DOES NOT ADD UP: … **` when the two do not account for `cycles`.
2. **teacher** — a new row inside THE BELT COUNTS ITSELF, `refused-words + allowed == cycles`, graded ONLY where
   the paper carries `allowed`; otherwise `[n/a ]` + "re-sail to grade it", never PASS. A `.3` paper still filing
   `(allowed)` → FAIL; a pre-`.3` one → WARN, because history is not rewritten.
3. **the vocabulary is deliberately NOT graded** — three words already exist and the committee may add more; a
   gate may own arithmetic, not a name.

**Verification.** `56 passed in 38.68s` on the edited tree, and `56 passed in 38.51s` on a tree REBUILT from
99b3cc8 + the four load-bearing patches + this one (`git apply --check` rc 0, stamps read back as `.3`). Law 52,
four mutations, four flinches: drop `allowed` from the equation · grade an old paper anyway · tolerate `(allowed)`
on a new paper · zero the sea's increment. The fourth is caught by a source pin, not by behaviour, and the
reason is recorded: **no fixture in this repo can produce a cycle that walks through** — `allowed == 0` measured
on the single-day tape fixture, on `_two_day_look_fixture` (61+61 cycles, every one refused) and on the
order-tape fixture, so the increment has no behavioural witness anywhere in the suite.

**The second finding was MY paper's error, not theirs.** 5 of 6 days printed `the summary says cycles=N ->
THE DIARY DOES NOT AGREE WITH ITSELF` (542 vs 137, 57 vs 19, 177 vs 99, 427 vs 6, 488 vs 61; only 11-06 agreed
at 124 == 124). Measured here on a real diary (244 cycle records, FOUR `replay:summary` records of 61 each): a
diary holds EVERY sail appended to it, and `summary.cycles` counts ONE sail. My label compared a per-run number
with a per-day total and announced a contradiction that did not exist. The block now prints the honest form —
`runs N: each run's summary vs its own records a/N, run-sums S vs day total T` (rehearsed on real multi-run
data: `3/3` with `218 == 218`, `1/1`, `4/4`) — plus a `why the gap` line. New block: 193 L / 11,207 B /
sha256 `5c78e3344586442d`.

**READ IT BEFORE TRUSTING ANY PER-DAY NUMBER.** The same per-run/per-day mix-up explains
`day_metrics_2026-11-06.json` = 176 vs diary 124 vs grid 208, and 11-05's 1042 vs 542: runs differ by window and
by `--days`, so a day's total is the count of its diary's cycle records — never one `cycles=` line.

**54. A FALLBACK WORD IN A TALLY IS A CLAIM, AND THE SUSPECT IS THE VALUE THAT LOOKS PERFECT (`2026-11-17`):**
`x or ["(allowed)"]` was written to be kind — "no refusal, so say so" — and it silently changed what the column
MEANT. A filler that names an ABSENCE must live in its own column, never among the things it is the absence of;
and when a tally's total EQUALS the count it is supposed to explain (here 6 days out of 6), suspect the filler
before the data.

**55. A NEW GATE MAY NOT GRADE PAPER WRITTEN BEFORE IT (`2026-11-17`):** abstain, say why, say what re-run would
grade it. A silent PASS is worse than no row, because that old paper would have passed the new equation *by
accident* — for the exact reason the gate exists. Same family as item 31 (`skips ABSENT`) and item 38
(`fwd9=None`).

### 3.5q ROW 3b SIGNED BY THEIR KEYBOARD, AND ROW 3c TAUGHT ME THAT MY OWN BLOCK APPENDS (`2026-11-17`, 03:00Z → 03:12Z)

**Row 3b is CLOSED by the owner's own run.** They applied `nov17_factory2_code_day11_tally2.patch` (their paste: `17698`
bytes, sha256 `4c6247b0227309cdd770` after CR-strip, `--stat` 3 files / `150 insertions(+), 13 deletions(-)`,
`check rc=0`, `apply rc=0`, both stamps read `…2026-11-17.3`) and ran the suite: **`56 passed in 142.60s (0:02:22)`**.
I checked their `--stat` against my file line by line (`gates_replay +29 -7`, `gates_report +35 -1`,
`test_factory2 +86 -5` = the 36/36/91 they saw), so this is a receipt, not a hope.

**Row 3c, the re-sail, is what actually grades row 3** — `nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh`, 284 L / 16,200 B /
sha256 `f8b1e4d80eca651d`. Design decisions, all forced by a lesson already in this Book:
1. **dials are READ FROM THEIR OWN PAPER, never typed by me**: `cycle_grid.window_start_utc/window_end_utc/every_s`
   + `warmup` + `rung_band` from the widest-grid paper whose `day` field matches, `--min-prints` being the one
   number no paper records (law 51b, the guessed window that invented 20 phantom skips). A `re-sail reproduced N
   cycles` line then proves the sail is comparable to the one it replaces; if it does not reproduce, the block says
   "a dial I could not read from your paper" instead of quietly accepting a different day.
2. **it writes new names only** (`gates_<day>_tally2.{json,jsonl,sea.txt,paper.txt,old.txt}` + its own receipt);
   old papers and old diaries are opened for reading, so the row-3 evidence stays intact.
3. **the live mirror is hashed before and after**, and the block aborts (`rc=4`) unless both stamps on disk end in
   `.3` — so it can never be run against pre-TALLY-2 code and print a PASS.
4. **`SKIP_SAIL=1`** re-grades what is already on disk and prints a warning that a PASS there is not row 3 closing.

**The finding this block taught me about itself: a diary APPENDS, a paper OVERWRITES.** My first rehearsal ran the
block twice and the second verdict was `[FAIL] diary 522 (allowed 0 + refused 522) vs paper 261` — not a defect in
the sea, but my own comparison treating a multi-sail file as one sail. That is law 53, aimed at my tooling: the
fresh check now reads **THIS sail** (records after the last `replay:summary`, which is exactly the per-run rule the
doorman receipt needed), prints `this sail 261 records | the FILE holds 1827 records from 7 sail(s)`, and asserts
every sail matches its own summary (`7/7`). Verified by re-running: the same sail count, `4/4` then `5/5` then
`7/7`, and a PASS each time — idempotence is now a property the block demonstrates, not assumes.

**And it was made to flinch (law 52), four ways, on a rebuilt tree (`99b3cc8` + the five patches):**
- *N1* append one diary record claiming a walked-through cycle → `[FAIL] the paper says allowed 0 / refused 261 /
  words 261 but the diary's last sail says 1 / 0 / 0` **and** `[FAIL] …do not describe the same sail`.
- *N2* edit the paper so it is SELF-consistent (`allowed 1`, words 260, `260 + 1 == 261` ✅) → the sea's tally and
  the teacher both say `[OK  ]`, and ONLY the new paper-vs-diary cross-check fails. That asymmetry is the point: a
  paper that agrees with itself is not a paper that agrees with its diary, and row 3 exists to catch exactly that.
- *N3* `DAYS=2026-11-05` with no tape → `NO ticks tape … -> NOT MEASURED (NOT MEASURED is never zero)`, never a zero.
- *N4* stamps `.2` → `BLOCK ABORT … rc=4`, with the wording corrected to what it really does (no sail, no paper,
  no diary — only the receipt line).

**56. AN EDIT YOU CANNOT POINT AT IN THE FILE DID NOT HAPPEN (`2026-11-17`, my own near-miss, one step from shipping
a decorative test):** a tool call returned `shell_error` with EMPTY stdout and I carried on believing its two edits
had landed. They had not — `grep -c "the paper's two columns ARE the diary"` said **0**, and the check I "tested"
against N2 was the one I had just re-inserted. Two rules follow: after any interrupted step, prove the edit by
reading the file back (a count is enough, a memory is not); and a check that was never inserted cannot fail, which
is law 52's failure mode wearing my own clothes. The same call-shape trap bit three times today: a heredoc inside a
long `;`-chain, and a `cwd` pointed at a directory the command deletes first.

**57. A TOOL THAT APPENDS MUST BE GRADED PER RUN, INCLUDING MY OWN BLOCK (`2026-11-17`):** `--json-out` overwrites
while `--diary-out` appends, so any "diary vs paper" comparison is a SCOPE choice, not an arithmetic one. State the
scope in the line (`this sail N | the file holds M from K sail(s)`), and let the run boundary — the summary record —
be what defines "this run". Law 53's shape-first rule, promoted from their diaries to my receipts.

### 3.5r HOW A `ls` ALIAS ATE AN ENTIRE STEP, AND WHAT MY "SHADOW" ACTUALLY IS (`2026-11-17`, the owner could not run the 3c block)

Their paste: `wc: '/c/Users/Jafar/Downloads/nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh*': No such file or directory` — a path with a
**trailing `*`**. Not a typo: Git for Windows aliases `ls` to `ls -T -F --color=auto`, and `-F` APPENDS `*` to an
executable. My wrapper built the path out of `$(ls …)`, so `$B` became `…sh*` and all four commands failed on a
name that never existed. The file itself was fine; my one line of shell was not. Fixed by never capturing a
listing to make a path:

```bash
B=/c/Users/Jafar/Downloads/nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh
[ -f "$B" ] || B=/mnt/c/Users/Jafar/Downloads/nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh
[ -f "$B" ] && echo "found: $B" || echo "NOT FOUND in Downloads - tell me"
```

**58. A PATH MAY NOT BE CAPTURED FROM `ls` OUTPUT (`2026-11-17`, one wasted round trip of the owner's):** `ls -F`
decorates executables with `*` (and dirs with `/`), and Git Bash aliases it ON by default, so `$(ls …)` hands you a
string that no longer names a file. Test with `[ -f ]` / `[ -e ]`, or `find -print -quit`; and when a step fails
with a path that has punctuation stuck on the end, READ the error - the decoration is the whole diagnosis.

**AND, WHILE ANSWERING "did we push?": my shadow clone is NOT the version we have.** Measured, not remembered:
`git remote -v` in it prints nothing (no remote configured); `git merge-base --is-ancestor 99b3cc8 HEAD` says
**NO**; and `git diff --name-status 99b3cc8 HEAD` lists `D committee.py`, `D factory2_gates.py`, `D test_factory2.py`,
`D tools/gates_replay.py`, `D tools/gates_helpers.py`, `D report_evening_gates.sh`, `A nov14_belt1_block.sh`,
`A nov14_eyes2_probe.py`, `A v8f_sail_recipe.txt` — i.e. branch `x3` carries the PAGES (Book 4,302 L, map, TODO)
while the CODE lives only on the `f2-tip`/`99b3cc8` line plus the five unapplied patch files, and `x3` still tracks
operator papers that were deleted from the shelf. **So "dirty 0" in my shadow never meant "current": a clean tree
only proves nothing differs from THAT commit.** Nothing of mine should be pushed anywhere; the authoritative
`.3` code exists in exactly one place - the owner's working tree, where `56 passed in 142.60s` was run.

**59. "DIRTY 0" IS NOT "CURRENT" (`2026-11-17`, BACKFILLED into this Book on `2026-11-17` 23:40 — the law was learned and written on the MAP the same night and never reached the Book, which is the failure it describes):** my shadow clone's `x3` carries the PAGES but NOT the `.3` code (`git diff --name-status 99b3cc8 x3` prints `D tools/gates_replay.py`), so a clean working tree proves only that nothing differs from THAT commit. "Clean" and "current" are different claims; say which one you measured. (Closed from the other side by `f70728d`: GitHub now holds the `.3` code, so the owner's tree is no longer the only copy on earth.)**

**60. A PUSH LIST COMES FROM THE IMPORT CLOSURE, NOT FROM THE PATCH LIST (`2026-11-17`):** "files I changed" and
"files the program needs" are different sets, and only the second one has to be true for the pushed repo to run.
So before any "let's push this version": walk the imports (a 15-line BFS over `import`/`from` matched against
files that exist), and `git ls-tree` the result against the remote tip; anything absent there joins the commit, and
anything the closure needs that is neither tracked nor added is a broken push waiting to happen.

**61. AN UNTRACKED FILE IS NOT BACKED UP BY A BRANCH (`2026-11-17`):** a branch/tag/copy of `.git` snapshots
commits only. While the Factory2 code was `??`, every "safety" git move the owner made protected the history and
nothing of the work - which is why row 3c and the push are both worth doing BEFORE anything else gets "cleaned". (The owner took the
first real snapshot the same evening: `cp -r data /a/gitHub/Gold-BookMap-data-backup-20261117`, (The owner then took the first real snapshot of `data/` and VERIFIED it rather than trusting it:
`find -type f | wc -l` = **1,697 on both sides** and `du -sb` = **8,166,835,113 B on both sides**, loop idle at
that hour, so it is a clean copy and not a torn one. Two lessons in those numbers: my `1,144` was `ls | wc -l`,
i.e. TOP-LEVEL entries only - `find` sees 1,697, and one must never be quoted for the other; and the copy sits on
the SAME physical drive as the original, so it defends against `rm -rf`, a bad edit and a future "clean up", not
against a dead disk.)

**The owner's `git add -An` dry run then saved the repo from itself, and from me.** Seventeen untracked entries at
the top level, and a blind `git add .` / `git add -A` would have taken ALL of them into a pushable commit:
`Workspace/26,09,16-23.20.bmw` (an IDE workspace file), six Oct-23/24 `.patch` files, `daily_audit_1111.txt`, a
`POWER-History-Backfill-Probe-0.3.2-refresh.jar` BINARY, and the whole `_attic_2026-10-17/` - hundreds of backup
trees, receipts and `.ps1` scripts from Oct 5-6, deliberately never tracked. That is what the explicit-path rule is
for, and the dry run (`-n`) is how you prove a staging list before you trust it.

**62. NEVER STAGE WITH A WILDCARD IN A REPO THAT HAS AN ATTIC (`2026-11-17`):** untracked is not the same as
unwanted - this repo keeps `_attic_*/`, jars, IDE files and old patches untracked ON PURPOSE, so `git add -A`
would publish a stranger's junk pile alongside the code. Order: `git status --porcelain` to see the set, `git add
-- <explicit paths>`, `git diff --cached --name-only` to prove what got in, only then commit.

**The same audit corrected my own closure list (law 60 was not enough).** The import closure names what the CODE
needs to run; it does not name what the FACTORY needs to be usable. `report_evening_gates.sh` (the morning-after
grader my pages keep pointing at, STEP 10 and the ladder) and `tools/v8f_payoff.py` (Room D's money ruler) are
untracked on their disk and absent from GitHub - so pushing the six code files alone would put code on GitHub whose
operator entry points exist nowhere. A push list is the closure PLUS every file a person is told to run.

**Their Book edits, measured before any merge:** `git diff --numstat -- HOLY_BOOK.md` = **224 additions, 1
deletion**, and the diff is 149,712 B against a 3,113-line committed base while my copy is 4,372 lines. So the
two files are NOT a fast-forward of each other: my copy adds ~1,260 lines of sections, theirs adds 224 lines of
theirs. The merge is mine to do (apply their hunks to my copy, re-check the numstat), and "just take the newer
file" would have destroyed 224 lines of the owner's own writing.

### 3.5u I FETCHED THEIR GITHUB, AND IT SAID WHAT NO LOCAL COMMAND COULD (`2026-11-17`, the owner asked: "did the updates land?")

My sandbox has no credentials and no remote of its own - but their repo is public, so an anonymous read works. I
added a throwaway remote, fetched, looked, and removed it again (`git remote remove` - nothing of mine can push).
What GitHub holds at that moment:

- `refs/heads/strategy-v2` = **`807ac51`** - "Gates check-up: laws verified vs research, wiring contract closed,
  fixes + tests 17/17". Inside it: `tools/gates_replay.py` **ABSENT**, `factory2_gates.py` **ABSENT**,
  `test_factory2.py` **ABSENT**. So **none** of Factory 2 was on GitHub - the owner had staged 8 files
  (`5,617 insertions, 6 deletions`, exactly the list I predicted) but had not committed or pushed. Staged is not
  committed is not published: three different words, and only the third one means anything to a second machine.
- `refs/heads/main` = `b7f4caa`, and `git rev-list --count strategy-v2..main` = **104**: their own main is 104
  commits ahead of the branch we work on. Flagged for them to decide, later; I merge nothing on my own initiative.

**And the audit of my own clone got uglier, which is the point of doing it:** `git merge-base --all HEAD 807ac51`
prints **NOTHING** - my pages branch and their `strategy-v2` have **no common ancestor at all** (my root
`db0bf52` Sep 17, theirs `a383220` Nov 11, "Stage L1b"). Two separate histories that merely share filenames. So my
shadow can never fast-forward onto their branch, and "I pushed it from my clone" was never even on the table: the
pages travel as FILES, and only as files.

**63. A PUSH LIST IS THE IMPORT CLOSURE PLUS EVERY FILE A PERSON IS TOLD TO RUN (`2026-11-17`, BACKFILLED: the Book carried this only as one sentence inside section 3.5s, while the MAP had it as a numbered law):** `report_evening_gates.sh` and `tools/v8f_payoff.py` are imported by NOTHING - they ARE the entry points - so law 60 alone would have left them out of commit 1 and the published repo would have had no way to be run. A dependency graph is not a usage graph.**

**64. CHECK THE FAR SIDE BEFORE SAYING "IT IS SAVED" (`2026-11-17`):** local green (`staged: 8 files`, `dirty 0`,
a passing suite) says nothing about GitHub. `git ls-remote` costs two seconds and is the only command that answers
"is another machine able to see this". A clone with unrelated history cannot even be offered as a fast-forward -
so ask the question in the order: *does it exist on the far side*, then *does it descend from it*.

**65. A GUARD THAT CRIES ON A FUNCTION NAME IS NOT A GUARD (`2026-11-17`, my own secret-scan):** I handed the
owner `grep -inE \'token|secret|passwo|api_key|account\'` and it hit six lines - all of them `day_token()`, a
harmless filename parser defined at `tools/gates_replay.py:339` (4 uses in the sea, 2 in the suite). Substring
patterns over a big staged diff produce noise, and noise trains a person to push through the guard. Anchor and
word-bound the patterns (`api[_-]?token`, `access[_-]?token`, `(?i)password\b`, `BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY`,
`hooks.slack.com`), and when a hit appears, SAY what it is in words instead of saying "stop".

66. **THE DOOR HAD ONE MOUTH, AND THE FIX IS A LAWN NOT A THRESHOLD (measured on the owner's two closed days, `data/v8e_sail_20261114.txt` + `data/gates_thu.json`):** 11 verdicts across 2026-11-12/13 - **every one `VERDICT=BOUNCE`, every one `PUSH=DOWN`, every one `TOLL=unpaid | EXCESS=c1/h3m`**, and 10 measurable ones went AGAINST the call (`0/6`, `0/4`; mean `fwd9 -4.18 USD` on a day whose ATR was 33-43, while price fell 3951.3 -> 3886.6). The cause was in MY law, not the market: `committee.py` read `if paid and brk >= 3: BREAK` / `elif (snapback or bon >= 2) and not paid: BOUNCE`, so an UNPAID toll - the first close beyond, the weakest moment a cycle can offer - was the ONLY state in which BOUNCE existed, and `attempt_fire` gates a ticket on ten laws with **the toll not among them**. Their own receipt is the proof the paid hours exist and go unheard: `TOLL=paid | EXCESS=c22/h66m` -> `UNDECIDED`, `c23/h69m` -> `UNDECIDED`, `refusal tally: NO-VERDICT=131, (allowed)=7`, day totals `toll_closes 29 / toll_minutes 87`. **A machine that speaks only when it has no evidence and stays silent when it does is not badly tuned, it is inverted** - so v8f does not loosen a threshold, it swaps the condition to `not (armed and not paid)`. Two WRONG readings of mine had to die first, both by measurement: (a) my `start-up artifact` theory (verdicts cluster in a day's first hour) was an ARTIFACT OF MY OWN CAP - `--max-minutes 480` ends the grid at win_start+480, so the 2-day sail could only show 2 of the 7; the uncapped run spans 369 minutes and disproves it, while the two shared episodes came out BYTE-IDENTICAL (`-2.4/1.4`, `-7.2/-7.4`) = determinism confirmed; (b) desk SIGNS are relative to the push (`v[desk] == -d`), so `absorb- tired` in a DOWN push are desks AGREEING with the fall - there were ZERO bounce votes on all 11 cards and BOUNCE rode the `SNAPBACK=1` trap stamp alone. A first attempt that only ADDED a requirement would have made the reachability set EMPTY (paid -> refused by `not paid`, unpaid -> refused by the new gate) = zero verdicts forever, which is exactly what my rehearsal's own pass-criteria were built to catch.

   _MERGED IN from the owner's own copy of this Book (`2026-11-17`, applied `2026-11-17` 23:2x): in their copy this stood as law 48. Renumbered to 66 because this copy had already used 48 for a different law - see section 3.5w. Their wording is verbatim, only the number changed._

67. **READ A RECEIPT BY ITS FINGERPRINTS, NOT BY ITS NAME (my own grep nearly called v8e broken):** asked to show the verdicts, I grepped the receipt tail, printed `fwd9 None` six times, and almost shipped a bug report against myself. Those lines came from `data/gates_sea_20261113_*.json` = artifacts of the owner's WEDNESDAY v8b run, which predates `lookahead_min`/`fwd_rows`/`measured` (`tools/gates_replay.py:808,820`, since `.9`), so `None` was the CORRECT reading of stale paper. Two bugs in one command: **(a)** `sorted(glob(...))` is ALPHABETICAL and old date-stamps sort first, so `| tail -30` returns the OLDEST files' tails; **(b)** a per-day file cannot carry aggregate facts (`day_order`/`mirror` live on `agg`), so reading one prints `None` for fields that exist. Laws now: read by **mtime** (`ls -t`) or by the exact name the run itself printed, and **always echo the path you opened** - `SOURCE: data/gates_sea_20261114.json` is what turned my wrong conclusion into a five-second correction. Filed not fixed: `report_evening_gates.sh:86` cleans only `gates_sea_${DAYC}.json` for the days it sails, so per-day artifacts pile up forever and every future reader inherits this trap.

   _MERGED IN from the owner's own copy of this Book (`2026-11-17`, applied `2026-11-17` 23:2x): in their copy this stood as law 49. Renumbered to 67 because this copy had already used 49 for a different law - see section 3.5w. Their wording is verbatim, only the number changed._

68. **A FIELD YOU INVENT ON SOMEONE ELSE'S DICT IS DEAD CODE (found 4 minutes before v8f shipped):** my first version armed the new law on `cv["shadow_law"]`, set in `gates_replay` right after `cv["symbol"]` - and their `f2.cv_from_snapshot_dict()` returns a HAND-WRITTEN whitelist, so the key was dropped on the spot and the law would have been armed NEVER, in a tree where every test of `evaluate_fight` still passed green. Caught only because I measured the bridge instead of trusting it (`"shadow_law" in cv` -> False, `cv["toll"]` -> absent, `cv["symbol"]` -> `REPLAY/2026-11-12`, and `f2.build_cyc` carries symbol/toll/trap) and then wrote the failure as a test (`test_the_mark_rides_a_field_the_bridges_actually_forward`). THE LAW: when a flag must cross a boundary you do not own, verify the receiving function KEEPS your key - and put the marker on a field that already survives, never on a new one. The same read also proved the LIVE factory cannot be reached by accident: `factory2_gates.py:340 import committee` -> `:409 committee.committee_evaluate(cyc, ledger)`, and a live cycle carries the broker symbol, not `REPLAY/`, so arming on that string is a hard wall, not a policy.

   _MERGED IN from the owner's own copy of this Book (`2026-11-17`, applied `2026-11-17` 23:2x): in their copy this stood as law 50. Renumbered to 68 because this copy had already used 50 for a different law - see section 3.5w. Their wording is verbatim, only the number changed._

69. **A NOTEBOOK TWO PEOPLE WRITE IN IS MERGED, NEVER COPIED OVER - AND ON A NUMBER COLLISION THE NEW ARRIVAL RENUMBERED (`2026-11-17`, the 224-line episode):** my owner laid my own printed copy of this Book over their working copy, which held 224 lines of their writing I had never seen. Nothing was lost ONLY because a `git diff` snapshot had been taken 19 hours earlier, on my asking, for another reason entirely. The rule: (a) before a shared file moves between our machines, they send me `git diff -- <file>`; (b) the merge runs THREE-WAY against the committed base, never side-by-side; (c) when both copies have a law 48 that means different things, NOBODY renumbers a cited law - the arrival takes the next free number and states where it came from; (d) the merge is proved by counting BOTH directions - every one of their lines present, every one of mine present, zero conflict markers - before a single file is committed. A file two people edit is a merge problem, and `cp` is not a merge.

70. **AN INTERPRETER MUST BE PROBED, NOT NAMED (`2026-11-17`, the rc=49 sail on the owner's rig):** my re-sail block asked for `python3`. On HP-240 in UCRT64 that name resolves to the Microsoft Store ALIAS STUB in `.../AppData/Local/Microsoft/WindowsApps`, which prints "Python was not found" to **stderr**, writes **nothing** to stdout, and exits **49**. One dead name then produced TWO different failures in the same receipt: the sail died loudly (`SAIL FAILED rc=49`), while the paper-search stage - which reads STDOUT of a `python3` call - got an empty string and announced the FALSE fact `old paper : none found with day==2026-11-06 - dials fall back to the signed recipe`. The silent one is the dangerous one: a tool that reports "absent" when it means "I could not run" has already lost the difference between an empty shelf and a broken lamp. Rule: never name an interpreter and hope - ask each candidate to run code (`-c 'import sys'`), refuse any path containing `WindowsApps` on sight, print the chosen interpreter and its `-V` INTO the receipt, and let the operator override with `PYBIN=...`. `python` is the real one on this rig, which is why `56 passed in 142.60s` and the doorman block never met this stub - the defect was introduced by ME writing a new block instead of copying the working one.

71. **A PICK MUST PASS AN OWNERSHIP TEST BEFORE ANY PREFERENCE IS ALLOWED TO TIE-BREAK (`2026-11-17`, 71b: `belt_a.json` beat the day's own paper on my "widest grid wins"):** my re-sail block looked for "the paper of 2026-11-06" as *any* json whose `day` field matched, then took the widest `cycle_grid.ticks`. A BELT-1 test artifact carrying that same `day` AND a `cycle_grid` with more ticks won - and the block would have printed `old paper : DOES NOT ADD UP` about a file that is not the day's paper, with a straight face. `day` is a CLAIM, not an OWNERSHIP proof; so is a plausible-looking shape. The rule: qualify first (name carries the day AND `printed_by` starts with the producer's own stamp), prefer only inside the winners, and when nothing is qualified, SAY `tier 2: WEAK SELECTION` instead of picking silently - and print the whole candidate table, with a REASON beside every rejection, into the receipt (`2>>"$R"`). A selection the reader cannot audit is a guess with a filename on it.

72. **A FILE ANOTHER PROCESS WROTE IS DECODED WITH A LADDER, NEVER AN ASSUMPTION (`2026-11-17`, the 0x97 that ate a completed sail):** on the owner's rig `python` is `/c/Python312/python` - Windows CPython 3.12.4, whose `sys.stdout` writes a redirected file in the **locale codepage (cp1252)**. The sea prints em dashes; byte `0x97` is not valid UTF-8; and my checker opened that `.txt` with `encoding="utf-8"` and died with `UnicodeDecodeError` at position 390 - AFTER `sail done : rc=0, paper written`. The evidence was on disk and the verdict was lost to a codec assumption. Rule: read foreign text through a ladder (`utf-8-sig, utf-8, cp1252, latin-1` + a replace fallback), which cannot fail loudly on a file that exists, and never let a decode error masquerade as a missing fact. If you must guess an encoding, guess a SEQUENCE.

73. **WHEN A FIX TURNS A WRONG PICK INTO NO PICK, IT IS NOT A FIX - an identity test may not borrow a completeness test (`2026-11-17`, my v4 over-correction, one sail later than the bug it cured):** v3 picked `belt_a.json` because I ranked candidates by "widest `cycle_grid.ticks`". v4 was meant to stop that, so I required an owning paper to have `day` + the three sea counters + a `window_start_utc` + an `every_s`. The last two are DIALS, not identity - and the day's REAL paper, `gates_2026-11-06.json`, was written by `.2` before the grid was complete, so it failed my test too. Result: `old paper : none found`, the belt line vanished, and my "fix" had traded a wrong answer for no answer. v5 splits the questions: IDENTITY is `day` + `cycles` + `tickets` + `fails` (prefer the name carrying the day, then a `gates.replay` stamp), COMPLETENESS only decides which DIALS are read, and the receipt says `of the four dials, 0 came from the paper and 4 from the signed recipe` so a silent fallback is never mistaken for a measured one.

74. **A READER'S FIELD NAMES COME FROM THE WRITER'S SOURCE, NEVER FROM A FIXTURE I BUILT MYSELF (`2026-11-17`, the key that has never existed):** v4 and v5 both asked a sea paper for `cycle_grid` - v4 as a qualification, v5 as the dial source. `grep -c cycle_grid tools/gates_replay.py` is **0** at `f70728d` (the published `.3` sea) and **0** at `99b3cc8` (the `.2` sea): the writer has never put that key on the paper, so my test could not have passed on ANY file the sea ever wrote - `candidates : 0 qualified` was arithmetic, not luck. `window_start_utc`, `window_end_utc`, `every_s` and prewarm are PARAMETERS of `replay_day(day_str, every_s, window, prewarm_min, ...)` and are NOT recorded on the paper either; what the paper DOES carry is `day`, the counters (`cycles`, `tickets`, `fails`, `allowed`, `errors`, `lines`, `prints`), the source facts (`src`, `depth`, `tape_scope`, `fed_by`, `cap_min`, `mirror`, `day_order`), the band (`rung_band`, `levels_seen`, `levels_kept`, `band_empty`), `skip_kinds`, and the stamp `printed_by`. A fixture I wrote proves my reader agrees with MYSELF; only the writer's source proves it agrees with the file. So: open the writer, copy the key names out of it, and if a key is absent say so in the receipt instead of printing a silent fallback.
**The Book merge was rehearsed first, then EXECUTED - the real numbers are in section 3.5w.** `git merge-file -p mine base theirs` with base =
`807ac51:HOLY_BOOK.md` (3,113 L), mine = my copy (4,399 L), theirs = base + the owner\'s `+224 -1`:
(1) with their side equal to the base, the merge returned MY file **byte-identical**, 0 conflicts;
(2) with three invented owner lines added in a region I never touched, all 3 survived, my 19 `### 3.5*` sections
survived, 0 conflict markers.
And the lossless direction was verified, not assumed: of the base\'s 3,113 lines, exactly ONE non-blank line is not
verbatim in my copy - the house-laws line, whose full 5,662-char tail sits intact inside my 6,844-char version
starting at char 1,182 (their line is 6,708). My copy is a content-SUPERSET of the base, so merging preserves
both; `cp` in the wrong direction is the only way to lose 224 lines of the owner\'s own writing.
