### 3.5w THE TWO BOOKS BECOME ONE - how the owner's 224 lines got merged in (`2026-11-17`, 23:2x-23:40, done on MY side, nothing typed on theirs)

**How it happened.** Their repo folder gained three files - "the 3 MD files, which were missed". The Book was not missed: it was already there, marked `M`, holding their own 224 uncommitted lines. What they copied in was **my** printed copy, the one that had been sitting in their Downloads since earlier in the day. Measured, not assumed: their working Book read `4,331 L / 884,921 B / sha256 37cf21eb711a1a5f` - that hash is my delivery - and `git diff --numstat` had moved from their `224 0` to **`1,219 1`**, the signature of my insertion-heavy copy. Their words were no longer in the working file.

**What saved them.** Their `ls -la` showed `book_local.diff  149,712 B  Nov 17 04:34` - the `git diff` I had asked for *before the push*, taken while their Book was still theirs. It reached me as `book_local_224.txt`: **the uploader here accepts `.txt` and `.patch` and has never once accepted a `.diff`** (measured: 77 `.txt`, 31 `.patch`, **0** `.diff` in `uploads/`), while size was never the issue - the largest file that has ever arrived is 340,617 B. Transferred byte-exact: `149,712 B` and `sha256 ccac414280b1e56f` on both sides, which is the only reason I trusted it at all.

**The patch, read before it was applied.** 3 hunks, **224 added / 1 removed** - so their Book was `3,113 + 224 - 1 = 3,336`, and reconstruction produced exactly **3,336** lines. Hunk 1 `@@ -14,8 +14,10 @@` rewrote the house-laws line and added laws 12 and 13; hunk 2 `@@ -543,6 +545,11 @@` five lines in the champions passage; hunk 3 `@@ -3111,3 +3118,219 @@` their 216-line appendix. `patch -p1` onto `f70728d:HOLY_BOOK.md` applied cleanly - and that base is exactly the ancestor their diff was taken against, which is why the arithmetic closes both ways.

**Of their 224 lines, 220 were already in my Book** - they had dictated them to me in chat and I had written them in. Only **4** were genuinely new: their laws **48, 49, 50** (`THE DOOR HAD ONE MOUTH, AND THE FIX IS A LAWN NOT A THRESHOLD` / `READ A RECEIPT BY ITS FINGERPRINTS, NOT BY ITS NAME` / `A FIELD YOU INVENT ON SOMEONE ELSE'S DICT IS DEAD CODE`) and one `- Code Day 3.9 (2026-11-14, Saturday, market closed…)` bullet. That is the whole shape of the danger: **overwriting would have destroyed 224 lines to gain 4.**

**The merge, run as a merge.** `git merge-file` with ancestor = the committed base, ours = my copy (4,467), theirs = base + their patch (3,336) came back with **4 conflicts, all in the tail, none of them a fight about content**: three were the **numbering fork** (my list had already used 48/49/50/51 for other laws) and the fourth was the end-of-file newline. Resolution and its reason: **their three laws keep their wording verbatim and take numbers 66, 67, 68**, each with a provenance line naming the number it held in their copy; **my numbering was not touched**, because prose on both sides cites it (the numbers cited in my copy: 10, 11, 12, 34, 49, 50, 51, 52, 60, 62) - renumbering 48-65 to make room would have orphaned every citation in the file. Their Code Day bullet went after my Code Day 3.8 bullet, which is where their copy had it.

**Proof before commit - the only kind that counts here.** Every non-blank line of their 3,336-line Book present in the merge: **0 missing**. Every line of my 4,467: **0 missing**. Conflict markers left behind: **0**. Laws 66/67/68 appearing exactly once each: **1, 1, 1**. The house-laws line: **one** line, **6,844** chars, and the base's 6,708-char version correctly gone - because **both of us had independently inserted the same BOLD-THE-MYSTERY law into the same sentence**, which is why it merged to one line instead of duplicating into two.

**Two things the merge exposed, both now repaired rather than smoothed over.** (1) The Book's numbered law list **skipped 59 and 63**: no line beginning `**59.` or `**63.` existed in it, and the text `"dirty 0" is not "current"` was absent from the Book *entirely* - both laws lived only on the MAP, and 63 existed in the Book as one sentence inside section 3.5s. A rule that only exists on a signpost is not a rule. They are now **backfilled in place** (59 before 60, 63 before 64), each saying it was backfilled and what the failure was. (2) My own MAP carried literal `✅` and `§` escape text in three lines from an edit that used a quoted string instead of a real character - visible garbage for six hours, fixed in this same pass. **A page I hand over must be read by my own eyes, not just written by my own code.**


**And the paper is PUBLISHED - read back from the far side, law 64 applied to myself.** Commit **`dc280b0`** (`2026-11-17` 23:49:28 +0100), parent `f70728d`, exactly 1 commit on top of it, and the commit touches **3 files and nothing else**: `FACTORY2_MAP.md`, `FACTORY2_TODO.md`, `HOLY_BOOK.md`. The junk check ran against the published commit's own file list (`^data/|_attic_|\.jar|Workspace|\.patch|daily_audit`) and printed **NONE**. Hashed FROM THE PUBLISHED BLOBS, not from their disk and not from my shelf: Book `4,503 L / 910,781 B / 173d420bbe7eb891`, map `565 L / 49,229 B / d74331a071e51317`, to-do `140 L / 17,579 B / d38745ea618fb61d` - all three equal to the bytes I wrote, so the merge reached GitHub whole. Inside GitHub's Book: section 3.5w x1, the owner's laws 66/67/68 x3, their provenance lines x3, law 69 x1. The paper commit left the code byte-unchanged: `tools/gates_replay.py d25523e70a02`, `tools/gates_report.py ccf9b30ba382`, `tools/gates_helpers.py 11b6ff088acd`, `test_factory2.py 98bab715bac1`, identical at `f70728d`.

**One number on my own map was wrong, and it is corrected here.** `FACTORY2_MAP.md` said "Book 4,490 L" - that was my PREDICTION written while the merge was still in flight, and the 59/63 backfill plus this section moved the real file to **4,503 L**. Numbers on these pages get written from `wc` / `stat -c%s` / `sha256sum` AFTER the edit lands, never from what the edit was supposed to produce; an unmeasured number on a page is a number that will mislead me a week later.

**And a number that looked like a mismatch was not one.** Their `git diff --cached --stat` printed `HOLY_BOOK.md | 1392` while my measured numstat said `1391 1`: `--stat` reports **additions + deletions** (1,391 + 1), `--numstat` reports them apart. Both agree, and the summary line they saw - `3 files changed, 2096 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)` - is exactly the figure predicted from my files (`1,391 + 565 + 140 = 2,096`). Predicting a command's output is only useful because it can be WRONG in a way that tells you something.

### 3.5x THE RE-SAIL THAT NEVER SAILED - `rc=49`, one dead interpreter name, two lies in one receipt (`2026-11-17` 23:49-23:5x)

**What the owner pasted.** The block found its file (`16,200 B`), stamped the tree correctly - `git=dc280b0`, the commit pushed four minutes earlier - both tool stamps read `.3`, the live mirror was `395a4f4c14506479 2026-11-17_22:32:06` **before and after** (so the block wrote nothing it should not have, and no damage was done), and then:

```
Python was not found; run without arguments to install from the Microsoft Store...
old paper : none found with day==2026-11-06 - dials fall back to the signed recipe
SAIL FAILED rc=49 - last 6 lines of the sea's own output:  <the same message>
```

**Diagnosis, from my own file, not from a guess.** Six call sites in the block asked for `python3`; every block that ever worked on this rig - the suite, the doorman, EYES-2d, BELT-1b - asks for `python`. `python3` is the Store alias stub: message on **stderr**, **empty** on stdout, exit **49**. That single cause explains BOTH lines: the sail never started (rc=49 is the stub's exit, not the sea's), and the old-paper lookup - a `$(python3 - ...)` capture - returned the empty string the stub prints, so my "widest grid wins, read the dials from the owner's own paper" logic fell through to the typed fallback. Their paper for that day exists; the doorman read 168 of them four hours earlier. **The receipt said "absent" when the truth was "I could not run" - law 70.**

**What the failed run left on their disk:** `data/gates_2026-11-06_tally2.sea.txt` holding only the Store message, because the shell opens the redirect before the interpreter dies. No `..._tally2.json` and no `..._tally2.jsonl` exist, since nothing ever ran to write them (the block then took its `[ ! -s "$J" ]` branch and stopped). That file is overwritten by the next run, and the paper search excludes any name containing `tally2`, so it cannot be mistaken for evidence. Derived from the block's code, not measured on their disk - one `ls data/gates_2026-11-06_tally2.*` confirms it.

**The fix, and how I tested it rather than typed it.** `nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh` is now **311 L / 17,860 B / sha256 `23061c37d6b5c2db`** (was 284 L / 16,200 B / `f8b1e4d80eca651d`): one `pick_py` added, six call sites routed through `"$PY"`, nothing else changed. `bash -n` returns 0. Then the tests that matter - a fake `python3` in `PATH` that prints the Store message and exits 49, and a `python` that works:
1. stub first, real `python` present - picks `python`, and the OLD-paper stage returns `data/gates_20261106.json`, i.e. it found the paper the failed run called absent;
2. that fixture had FOUR json files, and the pick is correct on all three rules at once: `day` must match, any name containing `tally2` is excluded (a decoy with `ticks: 124` was rejected in favour of the real `ticks: 208` - widest grid wins), and a deliberately broken `{ broken` json is skipped without an exception;
3. only the stub on `PATH` - the block ABORTS with exit 3 and a receipt line naming `PYBIN=/ucrt64/bin/python`, before any sail;
4. `PYBIN` set TO the stub - still aborts, because the choice is probed after the assignment too;
5. `PYBIN=/usr/bin/python3` with no `python` on PATH - accepted, so a rig with only a real `python3` still runs;
6. empty `data/` dir - `OLDP` empty, the fallback line prints, no crash;
7. my first attempt at the patch asserted 6 rewrites and found 7, because my own new comment contained the word - and it aborted **before writing**, so the shipped file never got half-patched (law 56 doing its job on my own tooling).

**What this did NOT change:** row 3 is still open; `.3` is still the code on disk; `56 passed` is still signed; the diary is still honest on 1,815 cycles. A round trip was spent on an interpreter name, which is exactly the kind of cost the rehearsal rule exists to catch - and this time my rehearsal fixture caught it in my sandbox, with the same stub I had to invent to reproduce their rig.

### 3.5y THE SAIL RAN AND THE GRADER DIED - two of MY defects, found in one receipt (`2026-11-17`, 23:04:09Z)

**The good news first, because it is the part that matters.** The `.3` sea sailed `2026-11-06` and wrote its paper: `sail done : rc=0, paper written`, on `git=dc280b0`, with the live mirror at `395a4f4c14506479 2026-11-17_22:32:06` identical before and after. Row 3 has now been SAILING correctly on real data; what keeps failing is my grading harness, and both causes were mine.

**Defect 1, the loud crash - `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0x97 in position 390`.** The interpreter the new picker chose is `python -> /c/Python312/python | Python 3.12.4`: Windows CPython, not MSYS2. Its redirected stdout is cp1252, so the em dash in the sea's own output is byte `0x97`, and my judgement stage opened that `.txt` with `encoding="utf-8"`. One fixed assumption turned "the file is fine" into a traceback that destroyed a verdict the run had already earned. It became **law 72**.

**Defect 2, the quiet lie - `old paper : data\belt_a.json`.** Not the day's paper: a BELT-1 artifact that happens to carry `day: 2026-11-06` and a `cycle_grid`, and my rule was "widest `ticks` wins". Under v2/v3 it beat the real file - and its `cycle_grid` was WIDER than the real paper's, so it won. (What the file on their disk holds, I have  never read: the finding is the mechanism, not their numbers.) Had the grader not crashed first, the receipt an `old paper : cycles N = tickets T + refusal-words W` equation computed from the WRONG FILE - an inflation story told about an artifact. The backslash in the path was the tell: Windows `os.path.join`. This became **law 71**.

**What v4 does.** `nov17_tally2_resail_block.sh` is now **388 L / 22224 B / sha256 `2ccb5204caf58970** (v2 was 311 L / 17,860 B / `23061c37d6b5c2db`). Three changes, all in the harness, none in the science: (a) every read of a foreign file goes through `rd()` - a `utf-8-sig / utf-8 / cp1252 / latin-1` ladder; (b) the paper pick is a THREE-TIER OWNERSHIP test - 0: name carries the day AND `printed_by` starts with `gates.replay`; 1: named for the day; 2: only CLAIMS the day - and tier 2 is refused in words, `WARNING: WEAK SELECTION - do not trust the old-paper line below`; (c) the candidate table, with `<< PICKED` and a reason beside every rejection, is appended to the RECEIPT (`2>>"$R"`), not buried in stdout. A pointless second `glob` also went, which had been emitting `SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence \*` into my own test output - noise in a receipt is a defect too.

**How it was proven, not typed.** A fixture was built that reproduces BOTH conditions: `gates_2026-11-06.json` (the real paper, `.2`, 124 cycles / 8 tickets / 124 refusal-words including the fake `(allowed)`), `belt_a.json` (the decoy, 900 cycles, 999 ticks), the block's own `_tally2` outputs, `session_levels.json`, a broken `{ broken` json, and the fresh `.3` paper plus a 124-record diary - with all three `.txt` files written in **cp1252**, em dashes included. Results: stage 1 picks `gates_2026-11-06.json` at **tier 0: selection is firm** and lists the decoy as the runner-up with the rejection reasons; stage 2 reads the cp1252 files without a decode error and prints `VERDICT day 2026-11-06 : ROW 3 ASSERTED on fresh paper`, rc 0; and the negative controls fire - remove the teacher's `[OK  ] refused-words + allowed == cycles` row from `paper.txt` and the block prints `[FAIL] the teacher did NOT print ...` and refuses to close row 3 (that FAIL was MY fixture on the first run, and it is exactly why the gate exists); rename every file so nothing carries the day in its name and the pick drops to `tier 2: WARNING: WEAK SELECTION` instead of guessing.

**What this does not change:** the dials v3 read from `belt_a.json` were `08:00-22:00 every 180 prewarm 150 rung-band 80`, identical to the signed recipe, so the sail that succeeded was the sail I would have run anyway; no number of the factory moved. And because `--diary-out` appends while `--json-out` overwrites, the next run's diary will hold TWO sails - which is why the checker compares the paper with `this sail N | FILE M from K sail(s)`, and why re-running is a test rather than a risk.

# ✂️ YES — 11 steps fit into 4 rooms (and the 11 stay as the checklist *inside* them)

You asked: *"is that necessary? we have all the steps separately from 0 to 3, can we put them in one or two steps?"*
Consolidating too far is what made this project hard to trust — every number had to be re-opened later. So the
answer is: **4 rooms to think in, and the small steps stay as the ticks inside each room.** Two rooms would
glue "what goes in" to "what we decide", and when a number looked wrong we would never know which side to read.

| room | swallows | one line |
|---|---|---|
| **A. THE EARS** | steps 0 · 1 · 2 · 3 | how a minute gets inside, safely |
| **B. THE JUDGE** | steps 4 · 5 · 6 · 7 | the 3-gate engine + the doorman — *the starting point of the whole design* |
| **C. THE BOOK** | step 8 | the only thing the factory produces: a line of paper |
| **D. THE READER** | steps 9 · 10 | the night teacher + the money ruler — decides whether B is worth anything |
| *(E. THE HANDS)* | steps 11-14 | **not built, on purpose** — order → position → manage → close |

# THE MAP, ONE STEP AT A TIME, WITH THE FIVE-YEAR-OLD EXPLANATION

## 🅰️ ROOM A — THE EARS *(steps 0-3)*

### STEP 0 · THE WINDOW — TWO PANES, ONE PARSER *(what the OLD factory's Room A actually does)*
You asked: *"check the old factory and see how Room A works there — I would like to read the previous days' raw
data AND the live market data from Bookmap through the bridge."* **The old factory already does exactly that, and
it is not two systems.** From `bookmap_bridge_provider.py`, the whole chain:
* the Bookmap add-on **appends** to `ticks.csv` (header `:53` `time,event,price,size,level,operation,instrument`) —
  the bridge is a **file, not a socket**;
* a **tailer** holds one handle and loops `pos = f.tell(); line = f.readline()` (`:668-669`), feeding every
  complete line to `self._handle_line(line)` under `self.lock`; an incomplete line is put back (`f.seek(pos)`,
  `sleep 0.05`, `:672-675`) — that IS tail-following;
* near a size cap it **rotates**: rename to `ticks_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.csv`, reopen a fresh header, kick the archiver
  (`_maybe_rotate:371-405`);
* the **archiver** gzips each chunk into `data/archive/`, **verifies by full decompression**, replaces
  atomically, then deletes the raw (`:423-441`);
* after a restart, `_catch_up:619` → `_replay_recorded_history:481` reads **archived chunks AND the still-growing
  live file, sorted by name, live LAST**, through the **same** `_handle_line`, and hands back the seek offset so
  the live tail is never re-read (`f.seek(live_offset)` `:630`); chunks older than the window are skipped by
  `st_mtime` **without decompressing** (`:527-540`); if the file is reset/replaced the tailer notices by **file
  identity**, resets every book and catches up again (`:678-692`);
* and the fake-order-id rule my sea had to re-learn is the provider's own `v5.3` line at `:334`
  (`if raw_oid in ("-1","0","","None"): return` — *"level=-1 causes 929 fake icebergs"*), which
  `tools/gates_replay.py:499` cites and `:639` obeys. **Same rule, two generations of the same room.**

**CORRECTION, made `2026-11-14` after the owner asked *"can we copy the exact work done in the old factory
into Room A?"* — and the answer is WE ALREADY DID.** The sea does **not** have its own parser:
`tools/gates_replay.py:765-790` does `import bookmap_bridge_provider as bbp`, builds a **thread-free** bare object
(`bbp._BridgeTail.__new__(bbp._BridgeTail)` at `:769` — `__new__` skips the constructor, so **no tailer thread
starts**, then the fields `_handle_line` needs are hand-injected), and feeds tape rows to **the bridge's own
reader**: `tail._handle_line(e_ln)` at `:881` — *"the bridge's own reader decides all"* — plus
`mbo_tail._handle_line(...)` at `:888`. `tools/raw_replay.py` (the old factory's own 4,998-line replay engine,
Law #8) does the identical thing at `:2186/:2263/:2293`, and its `tools/backtest.py:11` states the house
philosophy in one line: `ticks.csv -> REAL _BridgeTail parser -> REAL analyze_market()`. **In this house a
recorded day is re-played through the SAME live parser.** So Room A's PARSER is one copy, shared by old factory,
`raw_replay`, `backtest` and our sea — copying it again would be the ONE way to break it.

What is genuinely still ours, and genuinely still missing:
* **file discovery is re-typed, not borrowed.** Our sea globs itself (`gates_replay.py:67/79/100/116`,
  `rglob("ticks_*.csv*")` + sorted), while the old factory has `_discover_raw_files` (`raw_replay.py:165`),
  `discover_available_days` (`:271`) and a filtered chronological streamer `_iter_filtered_csv_events` (`:511`)
  that we do NOT call. It works and it is receipted (the belt counted every cycle), so this is a **parity note,
  not a bug** — but it is the one place Room A holds a copy instead of an import, and law N1 says the copy is the
  thing that rots.
* **the live pane enters at the top of Room B, not through Room A.** The hall's live loop
  (`factory2_gates.py:578-601`) reads FINISHED EYES from `data/factory2_snapshots/*.json` /
  `snapshots_history.jsonl` (written upstream by the old factory, `main.py:1093`, rotated at 50 MB `:1157`) — it
  skips the raw parsing. Replay reads cabinet rows through the bridge parser; live reads a snapshot the bridge
  already parsed. Same judge, same book, **different place where the minutes are born**.
* the two **clocks** differ on purpose: `raw_replay` evaluates on a **60 s** cadence (`--every 60`,
  `:4867`), our sea on the **M3 close** (`--every 180`, `gates_replay.py:1249`) — so our factory is NOT a copy of
  the old replay tool, it is a different judge sitting on the same ears.

> 🧒 **Old factory, one room, two windows.** The robot never plugs into the market — it watches a **notebook that
> keeps growing on the desk**. When a page fills, they **start a new notebook** and the old one goes to the
> **filing cabinet**. If the robot was away and comes back, it reads the **cabinet first, then the open
> notebook**, and remembers the exact page where it stopped so **no line is counted twice**. So *"yesterday"* and
> *"right now"* are the **same book, different pages** — which is why reading previous days AND the live market
> needs no new room, only a robot allowed to sit at the desk.

### STEP 1 · THE GLASS CASE (quarantine)
`quarantine_mirror()` copies the live `data/session_levels.json` into a sandbox and the run reads **the copy**;
the original is only ever stat'ed. Same timestamp in every receipt: `2026-11-13 22:24:10`, unchanged.
> 🧒 The robot has its own **toy box** and plays only with that. The real box on the other shelf is behind
> glass — it may **look**, it must **never touch**.

### STEP 2 · EYES + BRAIN (borrowed, zero copy) — and the room has **TWO freshness laws**, only one is measured
*(the finding of `2026-11-14`, made while answering *"confirm Room A is finished"* — it re-opened this step)*

| | **fire-law 1 `FRESH-EYES`** | **the sea's `stale`** |
|---|---|---|
| where | `committee.py:35` `FRESH_MAX_SECONDS = 15.0` | `tools/gates_replay.py:872` `if last_ep < tc - 240.0 or last_px <= 0.0` |
| what it measures | `age_s = now_ts - cv["ts"]` (`factory2_gates.py:217-221`) | time since the **last print on the tape** |
| used at | ONE place, `committee.py:364` | once per cycle, and it **skips the cycle** |
| in replay | **can never fire**: `cv["ts"] = float(tc)` (`:972`) and `now_ts = float(tc)` (`:975`) are the SAME number → `age_s == 0` **by construction** | fires 65 times on 11-06 |
| in live | real: the snapshot's own `ts` vs wall clock (the Book's `18.6s ages seen` receipt came from THIS path) | does not exist (no tape) |
| named knob | yes (`FRESH_MAX_SECONDS`) | **no — a bare literal, no flag, no sentence in the report** |

**Consequence, stated plainly:** `skip_kinds.stale = 65` in a sea json means **"the TAPE went quiet for >240 s"**,
NOT **"the FEED was late"**. The room's own vocabulary hid that: `gates_report.py:393-395` prints the words the
sea wrote, and the belt accepted them because the words are in its known set. Nothing was miscounted — but the
label invited the wrong conclusion, which is law 12's subject ("a field must not mean two things").
**So step 2's deliverable is NOT "fix the freshness rule": it is (a) measure what the 65 really were, then
(b) rename the word and give the constant a name + a report sentence.** Measure first — that is the order we
decided in advance.

`step1_data_acquisition` + `step2_market_analysis.analyze_market` (`:6167`): 18 eyes, judges' notes, L3
order flow, volume profile, VWAP, ATR, macro/news, POWER regime, KillZone, close-bar discipline. **Imported,
never edited** — law N1: the old factory is not touched.
> 🧒 We **borrowed the good eyes and the clever brain** from the big brother. Same eyes, same brain — we just
> did not take them apart. *Borrowed* means we can give them back tomorrow.
> 🧒 **Two clocks, one name.** The robot has TWO ways to ask "are your eyes fresh?" — the doorman asks
> "is the feed not older than 15 seconds?" and the tape-reader asks "has anything moved in the last 4
> minutes?" During practice with a recording, the first question **always** gets "yes, perfectly fresh"
> (we ask it about the tape, not about a feed), so the only one that can complain is the second. When the
> diary says **stale 65**, it means **"the recording was quiet for 4 whole minutes"** — not "the internet
> was slow". We were about to blame the wrong thing, so we measured it first.

### STEP 3 · THE BELT (one 3-minute bar at a time, with a tally boy)
Assembles `cyc` (price, atr, levels, notes, judges, toll, trap, l3q, oi, effort, prefire) and books every
refusal: `_skip()` names the cause (`no-eyes / stale / warm-up / brain / snapshot`) **and** bumps `errors`
in the same call (`:846-852`) → `errors == sum(skips)` is a law, not a hope.
> 🧒 The belt moves **one minute at a time**, and there is a **tally boy** with a notebook. Every minute the
> factory refuses, he must write **why**. If his numbers do not add up, something got lost — and a lost minute
> is how a robot learns to lie without noticing.

---

## 🧪 STEP 2 MEASURED — and my theory was WRONG, in public (`2026-11-17`, owner's `data/eyes_20261114.txt`)

**What I predicted:** `stale:65` on 2026-11-06 = "the market printed nothing for >240 s" (the rule at `:872`,
arm A). **What your tape says:** there is **no such silence on any of those days** — `max_print_gap` was
**56 s** (11-06), **31 s** (11-04), **33 s** (11-12). So `PREDICT stale=0 | tool actual=65` and the probe
answered **DIFFERS on six days, MATCH on three** — and the three MATCH days are the days where the tool
recorded **`stale: 0`**, i.e. they agreed only because both sides were zero. **My theory is dead.**

**What the failure exposed (read off the code afterwards, `gates_replay.py:864-873`):** the walk keeps TWO
variables, `last_ep` (last **print**) and `ev_i` (how many **rows** have been consumed) — and `no-eyes` tests
`ev_i == 0`, meaning "**not one row yet**", while `stale` fires on `last_ep < tc-240` **or** `last_px <= 0.0`.
A raw Bookmap tape carries **depth rows long before the first trade**, and in the tool's event list a depth row
is `(ep, 0.0, 0.0, line)`: it moves `ev_i` but never `last_ep`. So a cycle with **"depth arrived, no print yet"**
skips `no-eyes` (rows exist!) and lands in `stale` — **warm-up wearing stale's coat**. The other half is
arithmetic: `grid` runs to `cells_end` (23:00Z) whatever the tape's last row is, so every cycle **after the
tape ends** has a frozen `last_ep` and is `stale` too — a **frozen tail**, not a silence.
Your own numbers fit that shape: 11-06's first print is at **11:36Z** with `lag=216min`, and its last slice is
named `19:43Z` — depth-before-first-print plus a tail to 23:00 is roughly the 65 + 19 warm-up you recorded.

**Why my probe could not see it:** it measured **a theory, not the tool**. It built its event list from prints
only and never simulated the walk, so the depth rows that cause the effect were invisible to it. A probe that
disagrees with the thing it probes is not a finding, it is a second bug.

**Fix, and it is a rehearsal rule now:** the 2nd-generation probe (`nov14_eyes2_probe.py`, 200 L,
sha `1f575d369450…`) **replays the walk itself** — same check order, same row shape — and predicts **every**
class, then splits `stale` into **arm A (real >240 s silence after prints)** and **arm B (no print yet)**.
Rehearsed on a fixture built to hold both arms; its output equals my hand arithmetic exactly
(`arm B=20` for 50 min of in-window depth-only; `arm A=259` = 94 mid-gap + **165** frozen-tail cycles from
the tape's end at 10:27Z to cells_end 23:00Z; `grid=301`, `fed=21`, 301 = 279+21+1 ✓).
**If it MATCHes on your real days** → the fix is the check ORDER (test "have we had a print at all?" before
`stale`) and the two words (`pre-first-print` / `post-last-row`), **no threshold changes**. **If it still
DIFFERS** → the walk itself holds something neither of us has read, and that outranks any renaming.

> 🧒 I said "the diary calls a minute *stale* when the market was quiet for 4 minutes". **You measured it and
> the market was never quiet — 56 seconds was the longest pause.** So I went back to the code and found the
> truth: the robot also calls a minute *stale* when **the notebook has lines but no prices yet** (the book is
> open, the auction hasn't started) and when **the notebook ran out of paper** while the clock kept walking to
> 23:00. My first measuring stick was too small to see either one — it only looked for quiet markets. The new
> stick walks the **same steps the robot walks**, so it can only be wrong in ways I can see.

---

## 🅱️ ROOM B — THE JUDGE *(steps 4-7 — the engine we started from)*

### STEP 4 · G1 — "ARE YOU AWAKE?"
`committee.py:76 evaluate_mood`. Prints a mood card on **every** cycle, even the refused ones — "G1 NEVER
GOES SILENT". Flat lake / trend / chop decide whether a fight is even possible here.
> 🧒 The first question is not *what do we do*, it is *is anybody playing?* If the room is quiet, the answer is
> "wait" — and we still **write that down**, so nobody later says the robot was asleep on the job.

### STEP 5 · G2 — "WHICH LINE ARE YOU STANDING ON?"
`:175 build_rungs` + `:220 evaluate_location`. A rung = a real line (`whale`, `htf_poc`, `onh`, `value_edge`,
`ibh`…) that has been **tested N times**, **closed beyond**, and **held M minutes**. Your Mapper law lives
here: **only lines within ±$80 of price are drawn** (`--rung-band 80`; `0` = the whole map). No rung under the
feet → `G2-VETO`, and G3 prints `SKIPPED (no rung under the feet - waiting is the answer)`.
> 🧒 Standing on the edge of a crack in the floor is interesting; standing in the middle of the room is not.
> And "no crack here" is a **real answer**, not a failure — a good player waits for the floor.

### STEP 6 · G3 — "IS ANYONE REALLY FIGHTING AT THAT LINE?"
`:269 desk_voices` (the 18 eyes vote) + `:288 toll_paid` + `:296 evaluate_fight`. The **toll** is the law that
price must **close beyond the line and come back to be tested** before a bounce may be claimed. v8f flipped
one hole: acceptance now **opens** the bounce — armed only on a `REPLAY/` mark, so your live path is
byte-identical, and `factory2_gates.py:340` is the wall that keeps it that way.
> 🧒 People **say** they will defend the line. The toll is the **ticket they had to pay**: they fought there and
> came back. No ticket paid = no promise, just noise.

### STEP 7 · THE DOORMAN — the seven fire-laws + L3Q + ONE JUMP
`:355 attempt_fire` + `:337 TicketLedger`: fresh eyes · decide-at-the-close · fail-closed · L3 quality **before**
the trigger · look both ways (spread, news, position count, daily loss) · kill-switch · **diary required** ·
one jump per (symbol, cycle, gate). Any fail → `{"allow": False}` with the reason **named** (`NO-VERDICT`,
`NO-EDGE`, `G2-VETO`, `ONE-JUMP`…), never lumped.
> 🧒 Even a **good idea** must pass the doorman: are your eyes fresh, is the bar closed, is the door quiet, have
> you already gone today? He is not mean. He is the reason the robot can be left alone.

---

## 🅲 ROOM C — THE BOOK *(step 8 — where the factory STOPS)*

### STEP 8 · THE TICKET + ONE DIARY LINE
The card is written by `pre["diary"]({...})` — the pen is **injected** at `factory2_gates.py:307`, the committee
is called at `:347`, and law 6 says **no diary, no ticket**. One ~1KB json line per heartbeat into
`Workspace/data/gates_shadow_YYYYMMDD.jsonl`, **append-only, folded never trimmed**, and every *refusal* is
rendered too (law 4). ★ **This is the end of the chain. The ticket stays in the pocket.**
> 🧒 Whatever the judge decides — YES or NO — one **card** goes into the book. That is all the factory does.
> It does not run to the shop. It writes. That is why we can test it a hundred times and nothing can get hurt.

**Two seams that people get wrong** (they are why my bugs were catchable):
1. **The card is the wire.** The gates don't pass neat python objects along — the verdict is **rendered into a
   text card** and the teacher/`v8f_payoff` **parse the card back**. So a field invented on someone else's dict
   is dead code.
2. **The diary is the only output.** Paper out, nothing else. No money machine to break.

---

## 🅳 ROOM D — THE READER *(steps 9-10)*

### STEP 9 · THE NIGHT TEACHER — 12 gates
`tools/gates_report.py`: stamps must be in the signed vocabulary (11 words, `:42-56`; unknown ⇒ WARN `:167`),
LINES PARSE, LINE SIZE law (`MAX_LINE_BYTES = 1100`, `:40`), NO QUIET DEATHS, G1 never silent, G3 is a real
door, TWIN scoreboard, PER-EYE grades, CPU TASTE (`CPU_PCT_BUDGET = 0.3`, `:41`, gate at `:324`), footprint,
VERDICTS MEAN SOMETHING (the +9 min count) and, since belt1, **THE BELT COUNTS ITSELF** → `NIGHT GRADE:
n PASS / n WARN / n FAIL`.
> 🧒 In the morning a **teacher** reads the whole book of cards and asks: *did you count right? did you explain
> every no? did you stay quiet and cheap like you promised?* The teacher does not care about money yet. It only
> checks that the **machine is honest**.

### STEP 10 · THE MONEY RULER (read-only, new this week)
`v8f_payoff.py`: parses the cards back, buckets **P&L** per push / rung / toll / horizon, and cross-checks its
count against the teacher (`right n/N` at `--friction 0` must equal the teacher's `n/N`). Result so far: 3 days,
**53 paid-toll tickets, −0.19/oz mean → nothing pays at +9 minutes, either way** (Book 3.5a-h).
> 🧒 After honesty, we borrowed a **ruler**: if the robot had played these 53 cards, how many coins would we
> have? The answer: **about none**. A ruler, not a wallet — it measures paper from the past.

# 🫥 ROOM E — THE HANDS *(steps 11-14: NOT BUILT, ON PURPOSE)*

```
8 (ticket) ──✗──> 11 ORDER ──> 12 POSITION OPEN ──> 13 MANAGE (SL / TP / time) ──> 14 CLOSE = profit or loss
```
Verified, not assumed: `grep -ci "stop_loss|take_profit|SL|TP|chandelier|trail"` → **0** in `factory2_gates.py`,
**0** in `committee.py`, **0** in `tools/gates_replay.py`, **0** in `tools/gates_helpers.py`. The mindmap says it
outright: *"WHAT THE NEW FACTORY NEVER HAS: the executor van, the corridor, the firefighter's buttons."*

> 🧒 You asked for the last step to be *"closing a position in profit or loss"*. **We stopped the story one
> chapter before that, on purpose.** Because a robot that decides well but exits badly still loses, and while
> the hands were attached we could not see the judge clearly. So first we built the **eyes, the judge and the
> book**. The hands come last, when the judge is trusted.

Their shape is **already legislated**, so this is a *to-build*, not an *unknown*:
**entry = the 3 gates · protective stop = the boat's existing geometry · TP = frozen by Law #1 · management =
hands off · close = the diary writes the profit or the loss.** Evidence: the **ROPE CHAPTER FINAL LAW** — 108
fish-readings, ladder + control + elastic — *"a reachable promise + hands off beats every invented rope, in
every weather."* One file is still open: **STEP 2g**, the live boat's outward TP-extension hatch
(`position_manager.py:1710-1721`) that the frozen-goalpost law never reached; it must be re-graded on fresh
tapes the day Room E exists.

# 🌊 CAN THIS RUN ON THE REAL LIVE MARKET? (asked `2026-11-14`, answered from the code)

**Short answer: yes — and it was designed that way from the first day. This is not a back-tester with a
live mode bolted on; it is a live robot with a recording plugged in where the market usually plugs in.**

Proof, one line: `run_cycle()` is defined ONCE at `factory2_gates.py:337` and **all three doors call it** —
`live` (`:591`), `--demo` (`:576`), `--replay-jar` (`:561`) and the sea (`tools/gates_replay.py:974`, via
`f2.run_cycle`). The engine is not duplicated, it is SHARED. So a minute travels the same corridor either way:
`0` input → `1` glass case → `2` borrowed eyes/brain → `3` belt+tally → `4-7` the judge and the doorman →
`8` a diary line. **Room A is identical for live and replay; only its STEP 0 changes fuel.**

## THE DIFFERENCES, EVERY ONE (and 4 of them make live BETTER-informed, not weaker)

| step | replay (what we sail now) | LIVE (what it becomes) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 input | tape files, minutes per second | bridge/demo, must wait for each closed M3 (`time.sleep(max(30, interval))` `:601`) |
| 3 eyes freshness | `age_s = 0.0` + stamp `eyes:assumed-fresh` | **`age_s = now - cv["ts"]`** (`:219`) — a REAL measurement; fire-law 1 finally bites for real |
| 5-6 bar discipline | `bar_closed:assumed` stamped | true, the loop only ticks on closed candles |
| 7 spread / news | `0.005` + `spread:assumed`, `news:assumed-quiet` | **read from the feed** (`:230/:231`) — the doorman's "look both ways" stops pretending |
| 7 L3 quality | from `mbo_*.csv.gz` in the archive | **only if the bridge sends `level3_events`**; else `l3q:assumed` (`:215`) and the teacher must say so |
| 9 scoring | `fwd9`/`pb4` — the future is on tape | **no future yet at decision time** — a live minute can only be graded tomorrow morning |
| 1-2 safety | quarantine copy of `session_levels.json` | identical: same copy, same read-only, still nothing written |

## THE THREE THINGS THAT ARE NOT THE SAME (say them out loud, they are the whole risk)

1. **The v8f law is currently ASLEEP in live.** `grep -rn "REPLAY/"` finds ONE writer: `tools/gates_replay.py:973`
   sets `cv["symbol"] = f"REPLAY/{day_str}"`; the hall never does. So the flipped acceptance rule runs only on
   marked tape — deliberately, because your `factory2_gates.py:340` is the wall we agreed not to cross. It means
   **live and replay are not yet the same judge**: live is still the *old, stricter* one. Before Room D's numbers
   are ever used live, this asymmetry is a decision to sign — arm it, or keep live conservative and say so.
2. **A live day is ONE pass, not a hundred.** Replay can be re-sailed until the arithmetic agrees; live gets each
   minute once, in order, at market speed — which is exactly why every law here is written as *abstain and stamp*,
   never *guess*.
3. **There is still no Room E.** Live gives you cards and a diary, not a closed position in profit or loss.

> 🧒 **Yes, the same machine can watch the live market** — because we never built a "yesterday robot". We built
> ONE robot and, while testing, we showed it a **recording** instead of the window. Rooms A-B-C-D are the same
> rooms; only the window (step 0) changes. And live is actually **better informed**: the tape has to *assume* the
> eyes are fresh and the news is quiet, while the real feed can *measure* both. Two things still differ: the new
> law sleeps in live until you wake it, and nobody can grade a minute until tomorrow morning.

## HOW TO SEE IT WITHOUT RISKING ANYTHING (the ladder, already built)

`--demo` → one synthetic heartbeat, proves the corridor exists end-to-end · `--once` → ONE real live heartbeat,
through eyes → brain → judge → diary, and stops · `--live` → the loop, still writing only a diary, still no
orders, hall `--hall-off` → then `report_evening_gates.sh` grades the morning after, same as it grades a tape.
**Every rung of that ladder produces paper and nothing else.****3.7 CAN THE DESIGN RUN LIVE? (the owner's question of the same day — ANSWER: yes, and four of the differences make live BETTER-informed, not weaker).** `run_cycle()` is defined ONCE (`factory2_gates.py:337`) and every door calls it: live `:591`, demo `:576`, replay-jar `:561`, and the sea (`tools/gates_replay.py:974` via `f2.run_cycle`) — so Rooms A-D are the SAME machine and only STEP 0 swaps fuel. Live *measures* what replay must *assume*: `age_s = now - cv["ts"]` (`:219`) against the `eyes:assumed-fresh` stamp, and `spread_pct`/`news_blackout` (`:230-231`) against `0.005` + `news:assumed-quiet`. Live L3 exists only if the bridge sends `level3_events` (`:212-215`), else `l3q:assumed` and the teacher must say so. A live minute cannot be scored until the next morning — `fwd9`/`pb4` are after-the-fact by construction. **THE ASYMMETRY THAT MUST BE SIGNED before Room D means anything live:** `grep -rn "REPLAY/"` has exactly ONE writer, `tools/gates_replay.py:973`, so v8f's acceptance flip is ASLEEP in the hall by design (the `:340` wall) — a live day is judged by the OLDER, stricter rules until the owner wakes the law or consciously keeps live conservative. Room E (order → position → manage → close) stays unbuilt, so a live day yields cards, not money. The risk-free ladder is already wired: `--demo` → `--once` → `--live`, each rung writing paper only.
